# Lessons ledger — the compounding layer

`created 2026-07-06` · append-only · owner: kb-distiller agent (`.claude/agents/kb-distiller.md`)

**Why this file exists.** CLAUDE.md's rule is *invariants over memory* — but between "learned
it the hard way" and "it's a CI assertion" there is a gap where knowledge evaporates between
stateless cloud runs. This ledger is that gap made visible. Every hard-won lesson gets a row
the moment it's learned; its **enforcement** column tracks how far along the gradient it has
moved (`UNENFORCED` → `protocol` → `test` → `invariant`). `UNENFORCED` rows are the
kb-distiller's standing work queue. Some lessons are genuinely not assertable (venue
behavior, methodology judgment) — those record *why* and stay `protocol`/`ledger-only`;
that's an honest terminal state.

Rules: never edit or delete a row; supersede with a new entry citing the old ID. Every
lesson cites the finding/log entry that produced it (no claim without provenance).

---

| id | date | lesson | source | enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | 2026-06-18 | A synthetic/model price is NEVER a fill price. The pt1 trade lost 9.6% partly by treating synthetic prices as fillable. Every persisted number carries a source tag; untagged defaults to `synthetic`. | CLAUDE.md prime directive; `findings/2026-06-18-codebase-money-map.md` | **invariant** — Hard Rules #3/#4 in `scripts/invariants.py`; `core/source_tag.py` default; `tests/test_substrate_primitives.py` |
| L2 | 2026-06-18 | `pstdev` over ensemble members without `member_count >= 4` produces garbage spread estimates. | arb-bot post-mortem, inherited | **invariant** — Hard Rule #2; `core/stats.safe_pstdev` |
| L3 | 2026-06-18 | `ncep_gefs025` is byte-identical to `gfs_seamless` — including it double-counts a model and silently narrows ensembles. | arb-bot post-mortem, inherited | **invariant** — Hard Rule #1 in `scripts/invariants.py` |
| L4 | 2026-07-03 | Cloud sessions cannot `git push origin main` (permission boundary, not a race — proven by two clean rebases that still fell back). | L5 | 2026-07-04 | Kalshi's maker fee is 0.0175, taker 0.07 — a 4x difference. A first S13 draft charged every maker fill the taker rate; at mid-range prices the maker fee alone is ~1¢/contract, enough to consume a 1¢ edge entirely. Always take fees from `core.pricing.fee_per_contract`, never hand-roll. | `findings/2026-07-04-sports-maker-s13-verdict.md` | **test** — fee math pinned in `tests/test_substrate_primitives.py` / `tests/test_s13_maker_fillsim.py`; UNENFORCED as a static "no hand-rolled fee constants" invariant |
| L6 | 2026-07-04 | Bootstrap the independent unit, never the row: outcomes within one game are correlated draws — resampling by outcome fakes n. Same for strikes within a release, hours within a session. | `findings/2026-07-04-sports-clv-s7-verdict.md` (block-bootstrap by GAME) | **protocol** — encoded in probe precedents + `.claude/agents/edge-prober.md`; not statically assertable (unit choice is per-design) |
| L7 | 2026-07-04 | Never hardcode a bracket/strike width: BTC hourly brackets are $50-spaced (fine bands $100), ETH $10/$20. A fixed-$100 half-band check silently mis-scored every ETH hour. | L8 | 2026-07-03 | A "paired" reference price lagging the event by collector cadence is a confound, not a basis: S8's spot lagged settlement by mean 29 min, fully explaining the observed gaps. | L19 | 2026-07-06 | L7 (never hardcode a bracket/strike width) is genuinely NOT statically assertable: a numeric width literal is indistinguishable from any other constant at regex level, and the one live site (`scripts/s8_basis_probe.py` `BAND_WIDTH_DOLLARS_BY_SYMBOL = {"BTC": 100.0, "ETH": 20.0}`) is a deliberate documented schedule map — a name-anchored ban would either false-positive on it or have to sanction it, and the next hand-rolled width would just carry the sanctioned-looking name. | L22 | 2026-07-07 | `core/source_tag.py`'s `VALID_SOURCE_TAGS` (`real_ask`/`midpoint`/`synthetic`/`broker_truth`, per CLAUDE.md) has no slot for a resting bid's own genuine fillable price — a live order book has two real sides, not one. `collection/orderbook_depth.py` tags the ask ladder `real_ask` and the bid ladder `real_bid`, a value outside the canonical enum; harmless today (JSONL tape, not a DB row the invariant scans) but would trip `scripts/invariants.py`'s source-tag check the moment a `real_bid`-tagged value lands in a DB column. | `kb/lessons/00-lessons.md` L22 (this run); `findings` — see LOOP-QUEUE Q16 (2026-07-07) | **UNENFORCED** — kb-distiller to decide: add `real_bid` to `VALID_SOURCE_TAGS` + `scripts/invariants.py`, or document bid-side liveness as a separate tag namespace, before any bid-tagged value reaches a DB table |
| L23 | 2026-07-07 | An empty `yes_bids`/`no_bids` ladder on one side of a Kalshi order book is normal for a far-from-strike wing market (real one-sided book), not a capture failure — a depth collector must record it as valid data (`depth=0` on that side, ticker still counted captured), never as a DROP, or genuine market shape will spuriously tank `completeness_ok` on every wing. | `collection/orderbook_depth.py`, `tests/test_orderbook_depth.py::test_run_missing_book_still_captures_empty_ladders`, confirmed against a live Kalshi pass (LOOP-QUEUE Q16, 2026-07-07) | **test** — pinned in `tests/test_orderbook_depth.py`; UNENFORCED as a general "empty ≠ drop" invariant beyond this one collector |
| L24 | 2026-07-07 | L22 asked whether to add `real_bid` to `VALID_SOURCE_TAGS` or keep it a separate tape-only namespace. The "harmless today" half of L22's claim is no longer just inspection — a new regression test proves the existing DB-side enum check already rejects `real_bid` (or any non-canonical tag) the moment one reaches a `price_source_tag` column, so no live gap exists to close. `core/source_tag.py`'s docstring now cross-references this decision so the next reader doesn't reopen it as a bug. | this run; supersedes L22; `core/source_tag.py` docstring; `tests/test_invariants.py::test_db_real_bid_tag_is_caught_as_invalid_enum` | **test** — the DB-boundary safety net is now regression-tested by name; the taxonomy-widening question itself is **protocol** (terminal, Ryan's call, not a loop decision) |
| L25 | 2026-07-10 | A queue day-count check (e.g. Q7's "≥7 distinct days of `tape/crypto_hourly/`") must verify a `dt=<date>` entry is the expected **file** shape, not just that a same-named path exists. The 2026-07-08 main-rewind caused the collectors to briefly run a different (post-reset) code version that wrote `tape/crypto_hourly/dt=2026-07-10/` and `tape/sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-10/` as **directories** of raw per-market API blobs, not the canonical `dt=2026-07-10.jsonl` line format — a naive `ls tape/<family>/ \| grep dt=` day-count would have miscounted Q7 as newly eligible on unreadable data. 23 hourly passes (2026-07-10T00:26Z–19:24Z) affected; self-corrected the moment collectors picked up the post-PR#35-reconciliation code (confirmed via the first pass after merge, stranded branch `tape/hourly-20260710T1955Z`). | `findings/2026-07-10-tape-format-regression-crypto-sports.md` | **test / non-gating advisory (BUILT — superseded by L29, 2026-07-11; stale `UNENFORCED` marker corrected 2026-07-21 by the kb-distiller, lesson text unchanged)** — implemented as `scripts/invariants.py`'s `_tape_dir_shape_issues()`/`tape_dir_shape_warning()` (~L433-466), the non-gating `--full` advisory that flags any `tape/<family>/dt=<date>` path that is a directory instead of the canonical `.jsonl`; pinned by `tests/test_invariants.py::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_finds_directories` (+siblings). Original candidate (preserved): an invariants/tape-audit check that a `dt=<date>` path is a regular file, not a directory, per known tape family. |
| L26 | 2026-07-11 | Kalshi's 1¢ minimum YES tick mechanically mirrors into a **\$1.00 NO ask** on any genuinely-far / floor-pinned bracket: `yes_ask` at the 1¢ floor ⇒ `yes_bid = 0` ⇒ `no_ask = 1 − yes_bid = \$1.00`. A **taker** "fade the rich tail" trade therefore has NO fillable positive-EV price on that side — profit is capped at exactly \$0 before fees (and `fee_per_contract(\$1.00) = 0`). In S10's tape only 4/18,992 (0.02%) far observations had any `no_ask < \$1.00` room, 3 of the 4 from a single hour. Any real tail-fade edge must live on the **maker** side (rest a NO offer / sell the rich YES *at* the elevated ask), which is S6/S11 territory (maker fee 4× cheaper) and needs L2 depth + a fill-sim — not falsified by a taker cut. Generalizes L12's fine-band floor-artifact caution to the NO / fade direction. | `findings/2026-07-11-crypto-reachability-s10-firstcut.md` (S10 structural DEAD, verifier-CONFIRMED) | **ledger-only** — venue microstructure fact (the mirror is arithmetic: `no_ask = 1 − yes_bid`); cross-references L12's `tests/test_anomaly_sweep.py` floor-artifact test. The "tail-fade is structurally a maker trade" corollary is future-probe discipline (addressed to S6/S11), not statically assertable. |
| L27 | 2026-07-11 | `core.pricing.fee_per_contract(1.00) == 0` is **correct, not a bug** (a \$1.00 fill has no fee), but a block-bootstrap dominated by ~\$1.00-priced legs can yield a 95% CI whose lower bound is a floating-point **+0.000000** — technically "not negative" yet economically meaningless. S10: mean **+\$0.000008**, 95% CI **[+\$0.000000, +\$0.000024]** — three orders of magnitude below the 1¢ minimum tick (~0.001¢), i.e. unfillable rounding residue of a few floored trades. A sign-only CI check lets a mechanically-floored population masquerade as "alive"; every CI-based verdict must ALSO clear an **economic-significance / magnitude gate** (compare the estimate and CI bounds against the 1¢ tick), not just strictly-positive-on-sign. | `findings/2026-07-11-crypto-reachability-s10-firstcut.md` | **UNENFORCED** — candidate: a magnitude-vs-tick assertion in a shared bootstrap-verdict helper (no such shared helper exists yet — each probe rolls its own CI); kb-only this milestone (scope limited to `kb/`). Not statically assertable at the invariants layer (it is a per-verdict methodology gate), so likely terminal as **protocol** once a probe-precedent encodes it. |
| L28 | 2026-07-11 | Before running a decay/CI pipeline to satisfy a gate like "clear the artifact floor," first verify the floor is even **observable** in the data: check what fraction of the candidate population is ALREADY floor-pinned at the **earliest** observation. S10's far brackets sat at the 1¢ YES floor ~40 min before close (mean early→late Δ`yes_ask` **+0.00014** across 18,992 obs) — there was no decay window beneath the floor to measure, a fact the probe surfaced only after building the whole EARLY/LATE-capture pipeline. A cheap early floor-pinned-fraction precheck would have surfaced this before the full build. Same discipline family as L6 (bootstrap the right unit) and L13 (exclude the startup artifact): descriptive sanity checks precede the verdict machinery. | `findings/2026-07-11-crypto-reachability-s10-firstcut.md`, `scripts/s10_reachability_probe.py` | **UNENFORCED** — candidate: a reusable first-observation floor-pinned-fraction precheck as probe hygiene; not statically assertable as one invariant (per-probe population choice, like L6), so likely terminal as **protocol** encoded in probe precedents / `.claude/agents/edge-prober.md`. kb-only this milestone. |
| L29 | 2026-07-11 | L25's candidate (an invariants/tape-audit check that a `dt=<date>` path is a regular file, not a directory) is now built: `scripts/invariants.py`'s `_tape_dir_shape_issues()`/`tape_dir_shape_warning()`, wired into `--full`'s stderr output alongside the L20 stranded-tape advisory (same non-gating pattern — offline-safe, never flips the exit code). Live-validated against the real committed tree: it correctly flags the 4 stray directories the 2026-07-08 regression left behind (`crypto_hourly/dt=2026-07-10`, `sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-02`, `sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-09`, `sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-10`) that L25 first documented — those directories were never cleaned up after the regression self-corrected, only the newly-written data went back to the canonical format. Cleaning up the stray directories themselves (or reprocessing their raw blobs) is separate follow-up work, out of scope for this milestone. | this run; supersedes L25; `scripts/invariants.py`; `tests/test_invariants.py::test_tape_dir_shape_warning_none_when_empty`, `::test_tape_dir_shape_warning_message_content`, `::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_finds_directories`, `::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_clean_tree_is_empty`, `::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_missing_tape_root_is_empty`, `::test_tape_dir_shape_warning_never_gates_exit_code` | **invariant (non-gating advisory)** — fires automatically on every `invariants.py --full` run; supersedes L25's `UNENFORCED` |
| L30 | 2026-07-11 | Kalshi's maker fee is a **flat $0.01/contract at every interior price**, not price-scaled: `fee_per_contract(P, MAKER_FEE_RATE) = ceil(0.0175·P·(1−P)·100)/100 = 0.01` for all `0<P<1`, because `max P(1−P) = 0.25` ⇒ `0.0175·0.25·100 = 0.4375` always ceils to 1. Consequence: a maker must net **more than a full cent** of half-spread-minus-adverse-selection to break even, so the modal 1–2¢-spread Kalshi book (0.5–1¢ half-spread) is structurally unprofitable to quote *before* adverse selection is even counted — this is the structural killer of S6 (spread-capture) and the same fee-floor that killed S13 (the maker fee ate the assumed 1¢ bid-under-fair margin). Sharpens L5/L18's "4× cheaper than taker" framing into a hard 1¢ floor. | `findings/2026-07-11-mm-spread-s6-firstcut.md` (S6 first-cut DEAD, verifier-CONFIRMED) | **test** — pinned by `tests/test_s6_maker_firstcut.py::test_maker_fee_is_flat_one_cent_at_every_interior_price` (value-sweep over `p∈[0.01,0.99]`, only distinct value 0.01). Best home is that value-sweep test, not a static invariant: flatness is a numeric property of `core.pricing.fee_per_contract`, and `scripts/invariants.py` scans source *text* — it cannot evaluate the fee function, so a static assert here would be dishonest theater. Extends L5/L18. |
| L31 | 2026-07-11 | A **wide (>30¢) bid-ask spread on a far / one-sided Kalshi bracket is a nominal, not maker-capturable, spread** — it is wide *because* there is no two-sided interest (e.g. a 0.03 bid against a 0.89 ask). A naive maker-spread P&L that books the half-spread as income lets these unfillable wings dominate the mean: S6's >30¢-wing population showed **+$0.339/contract** (99.9% "profitable") vs **−$0.002** on realistic ≤10¢ two-sided books, and the naive "ALL two-sided" mean (+$0.06928) was *entirely* this wing artifact. Any maker-P&L calc must cap the spread / exclude the wing before a verdict — declaring the wing "alive" repeats the L12 (arb-direction) and L26 (taker fade / $1.00-NO-ask direction) floor-artifact errors, now generalized to the **spread-capture** direction. | `findings/2026-07-11-mm-spread-s6-firstcut.md` | **ledger-only** — venue microstructure fact (a far bracket quotes a wide spread precisely because one side of its book is empty). The actionable corollary (exclude/cap wing spreads before a maker-edge verdict) is a per-probe methodology gate, not statically assertable (the fillable-population cut is per-design, same class as L6's bootstrap-unit choice); cross-references L12's `tests/test_anomaly_sweep.py` floor-artifact test. kb-only this milestone. |
| L32 | 2026-07-11 | For a maker-spread proxy built over **hourly BBO snapshots**, a *frozen* consecutive pair (bid AND ask unchanged between two captures) is a **no-fill**, not free spread income — booking its nominal half-spread as riskless profit (`dmid=0` ⇒ `net = half_spread − fee`) is the naive error that inflates a phantom maker edge. Report the frozen fraction as an L28-style observability precheck (S6: 25,618/36,738 = **69.7%** of ≤90-min consecutive two-sided pairs frozen) and bracket every verdict with BOTH a frozen-inclusive cut (maximally generous — counts unrealized spread as income) AND a movement-conditioned cut (the only population where a fill plausibly occurred and adverse selection is real). S6's DEAD is robust precisely because both ends came out strictly negative (≤10¢ frozen-inclusive CI [−$0.00297,−$0.00094]; movement-conditioned CI [−$0.02271,−$0.01759]) — the kill survives either fill assumption. | `findings/2026-07-11-mm-spread-s6-firstcut.md`, `scripts/s6_maker_firstcut.py` | **UNENFORCED** — a per-probe methodology gate (frozen-fraction precheck + dual-cut bracketing), same family as L28 (verify the artifact floor is observable first) and L6 (bootstrap the right unit); not statically assertable as one invariant because the population and fill assumption are per-design. Likely terminal as **protocol** once encoded in probe precedents / `.claude/agents/edge-prober.md`. kb-only this milestone (scope limited to `kb/`). |

| L39 | 2026-07-13 | When a bracket-ladder P&L is a **small net of two large legs** (Σ collected premium ≈ the \$1 payout — S14's mean +\$0.0925 sits on top of a ~\$0.51–\$0.97 winner-\$1 loss), a **candlestick/volume fill proxy** (`high ≥ ask AND volume > 0`) that credits the **income leg without queue position is biased UPWARD**: a bar only proves the price *printed*, not that a resting offer ahead of the whole book would have filled. The loss leg is NOT so biased (heavy volume sweeps the offer — S14 winner filled 96.7%), so the bias is **asymmetric** and inflates the net. A per-leg **volume gate** (require the income leg to carry volume ≥ X) is **necessary-but-INSUFFICIENT** — it caps thin-print optimism but cannot model the **adverse-selection correlation** between *which* income strikes fill and *which* strike wins (which the queue-blind proxy assumes independent). Before claiming fillability, **decompose the edge as a fraction of the thinnest income legs**: S14's \$0.072 of \$0.093 (**78%**) came from sub-100-contract-volume legs, and the "complete fill" gate term was exactly \$0 (0.0% complete-fill) — the fat nominal overround never underwrites; the edge is entirely near-money partial pass-through. Family L27 (magnitude gate) / L30 (flat 1¢ fee annihilates 30.9% of the nominal overround) / L31 (nominal ≠ capturable) / L32 (frozen-vs-movement dual-cut). | `findings/2026-07-13-ladder-underwriting-s14-firstcut.md` (S14 first cut, verifier CONFIRMED-WITH-CAVEAT) | **UNENFORCED** — a per-probe verdict-methodology gate (the income-leg decomposition + queue-blind-proxy caution), same class as L6/L27/L28/L32: the population cut and fill assumption are per-design, so it is **not statically assertable** at the invariants layer. Not pinnable by a unit test either (it is a judgment about how to read a proxy, not a numeric property of a function). Terminal as **protocol** once a probe-precedent/edge-prober house-style encodes "decompose a small-net-of-two-legs edge by income-leg volume before calling it fillable" — an importable helper (à la L33/L35's bootstrap functions) would live in `core/`, outside this distiller's lane; flagged for a future run with core-write scope. The queue-aware L2 fill-sim named as S14's remaining binding gate is the real fix. |

| L41 | 2026-07-13 | **Degenerate bootstrap: when every resample cluster resolves the same direction (zero losing clusters), a one-sided cluster-bootstrap p is MECHANICALLY 0 and carries no evidentiary weight** — no resample can produce a pooled mean ≤ 0 if no unit is ≤ 0, so p=0.0 regardless of the population's true edge. S20's sole BH-FDR survivor (`afkpnlucl`, mean e +6.6¢, boot CI90 [+1.3¢,+9.3¢], n=174) drew all 174 fills from **8 market clusters, every one of which resolved to 0** (the wallet sold longshots that all lost) — the "significance" was a construction artifact, and the catastrophic leg (a sold longshot that *wins*, −89¢ at this median) was structurally absent because the eval set conditions on markets already RESOLVED during the sample, and in a long-horizon winner ladder the early resolutions are disproportionately longshot eliminations. Root-cause family = **resolution-conditioning bias** (conditioning long-horizon skill evaluation on already-resolved markets truncates the tail that hasn't resolved yet — the wallet's still-alive book, Spain/England/France, was exactly the unpriced tail). Any positive-edge bootstrap claim requires **≥1 losing (opposite-sign-mean) cluster** in the resample unit AND a minimum cluster count (8 is below any comfortable floor — cf. S19's 2-event-hour filled population, L-family: a positive magnitude-gate-clearing CI over a below-floor unit count is a resampling artifact, not an edge). | `findings/2026-07-13-polymarket-wallet-forensics-s20-dossier.md` (Result 2, verifier finding; §Peer review); `findings/2026-07-13-polymarket-wallet-forensics-s20-prereg.md` (C1 cluster-bootstrap spec) | **helper + test (2026-07-13, same-day core-write pass, Ryan-approved local session)** — `core.bootstrap.bootstrap_verdict_admissible(unit_values, *, min_units=10)`: inadmissible unless ≥1 unit's mean sits strictly opposite the pooled mean's sign AND ≥`min_units` non-empty units; returns a reasons dict ({below_min_units, no_opposing_unit, empty}) so verdict reports record WHY, sibling to `clears_tick_magnitude` (L27) in the verdict rule — a CI failing EITHER gate is not-a-verdict by construction. 8 pinned tests in `tests/test_bootstrap.py` (incl. the exact S20 8/8-winning-clusters shape and S19's 2-unit shape). Like L27/L33 this is opt-in-by-import, not a static invariant (same runtime-vs-text reason); probe charters (L34) + Q24's binding gate make it protocol-mandatory for new probes. Until then the resolution-conditioning half is a per-probe methodology gate (population choice is per-design, L6-class) and stays **protocol** once encoded — the S20 dossier + Q24's probe spec ("explicit negative-skew accounting: the sold-longshot-wins leg modeled, not conditioned away; the ≥1-losing-cluster floor enforced") already carry it forward. |

Closing any of these needs an AST/dataflow pass, not a wider regex; an honest documented hole beats a false-positive-prone guard. | L51 | 2026-07-14 | A "does signal X beat the mid" calibration precheck run on a DISAGREEMENT subset (both directional, X's call ≠ mid's call) produces two hit rates that are mechanically COMPLEMENTARY on a two-way market (`hit_X ≡ NOT hit_mid`, sum to exactly 1.0) — they are not two independent measurements. Q26/S22's disagreement-subset hit rate (imbalance 27.9% vs mid 72.1%) looked suspicious ("far below 50%, is the sign flipped and this a hidden contrarian edge?") until the verifier confirmed the arithmetic identity: X can only "beat the mid" on this subset if the mid is <50% accurate exactly where they disagree — a bar a liquid, calibrated market essentially never fails, and sign-flipping X on this subset just reproduces betting the mid (zero independent edge either direction). Any future "feature beats the market price" probe on a 2-way market should report the disagreement-subset result as "mid accuracy where they disagree = X%", not as two independent hit rates, to avoid the illusion of an extra data point. | `findings/2026-07-14-ofi-depth-imbalance-s22-verdict.md` (Q26/S22 verdict, verifier-surfaced) | **UNENFORCED** — a statistical-interpretation discipline for calibration-precheck-style probes, not statically assertable (whether a given probe's "disagreement subset" is a strict two-way partition is per-design); candidate: `.claude/agents/edge-prober.md` house-style note for any future calibration-precheck milestone. |
| L52 | 2026-07-14 | Kalshi sports settlement results are not always binary: Q26's live pull of `fetch_kalshi_settled` over 458 settled markets in 7 sports series returned 8 with `result:"scalar"`, not `result ∈ {"yes","no"}`. A binary-outcome probe must filter explicitly on the result field's actual value, never assume "settled" implies "yes/no" — an unfiltered join would silently inject non-binary rows into a yes/no hit-rate or P&L calculation. | `findings/2026-07-14-ofi-depth-imbalance-s22-verdict.md` (Q26/S22); `scripts/q26_ofi_depth_imbalance_probe.py` | **helper + test + non-gating advisory (2026-07-25 idle-run enforcement pass; supersedes the earlier UNENFORCED marker per the L152 own-row-update rule, L25/L47/L123/L152 precedent)** — three layers, all built this pass. **(1) READ-side helper** `core/settlement.py` — the shared classifier this row asked for: `BINARY_RESULTS`/`VALID_BINARY_RESULTS`, `normalize_result`, `is_binary_result`, `binary_outcome` (returns `None`, never a fabricated `0`, for a non-binary value), `require_binary_result`, `filter_binary_settlements(rows, *, result_key="result")` / `filter_binary_results_map(results)` returning a frozen `BinaryFilterReport` (`total`/`kept`/`dropped`/`dropped_by_result`/`kept_fraction`/`summary()`, keyed on the RAW un-normalized value for diagnostic fidelity). Classification is a strict ALLOW-list (`normalize_result(x) in VALID_BINARY_RESULTS`), so an unknown future result string is non-binary by default — this row's bug with a new label in it cannot recur; `KNOWN_NON_BINARY_RESULTS = {"scalar"}` is documentation only and is never consulted by the classifier. `"void"` was deliberately NOT invented (`grep -rn '"void"' kb/ collection/ scripts/ tape/` → 0 hits, 2026-07-25). **(2) tests** `tests/test_settlement_binary_filter.py` — **61 passed** (`python -m pytest -q tests/test_settlement_binary_filter.py`), including `test_regression_l52_unfiltered_hit_rate_differs_from_filtered` (an unfiltered yes/no hit-rate numerically differs from the filtered one) and the HARD acceptance test `test_acceptance_1_l52_real_settlement_ledger_tape_is_all_binary` over the real committed `tape/settlement_ledger/*.jsonl`. **(3) non-gating advisory** `scripts/invariants.py::_handrolled_binary_result_sites` / `handrolled_binary_result_warning`, wired into `main()`'s whole-tree (`--full`/default) branch ONLY — never `--pre-edit-hook`, never `--db` — inside an `except BaseException` wrapper, stderr-only, never appended to the gating `failures` list; `HANDROLLED_BINARY_RESULT_EXEMPT = ("core/settlement.py",)`. Detection: an `==`/`!=` against a `'yes'`/`'no'` string literal (either operand order) sharing ONE line with a settlement token (`result`/`results`/`settle*`/`outcome`); a file is exempted wholesale if it contains a `"scalar"` literal, an explicit `in`/`not in` over a 2-element yes/no collection, or any `core.settlement` reference; docstring lines excluded via an `ast` pass, comment lines and `tests/` skipped. Pinned by **64 tests** in `tests/test_settlement_result_advisory.py` (`python -m pytest -q tests/test_settlement_result_advisory.py`) — 8 constructed positives, 17 constructed negatives, 4 regression-pinned blind-spot MISSES, robustness cases incl. an unparseable file, 7 `test_acceptance_*` real-tree cases, 7 exit-code/non-gating/offline pins. **Live measured (real tree, 2026-07-25):** the advisory reports **6 sites** — `scripts/s14_ladder_fillsim.py:119`, `scripts/s19_wing_fade_fillsim.py:142`, `scripts/seed5_funding_prior_probe.py:180`, `scripts/weather_rehab_s5.py:508`/`:610`/`:616` (the first three all the MECE-ladder shape `winners = [k for k, v in results.items() if v == "yes"]`) — and `python scripts/invariants.py --full` still exits **0**. Ten sibling probes (`longshot_fade_probe.py`, `q24_*`, `q26_*`, `q27_*`, `q28_*`, `q29_*`, `q30_*`, `q37_*`, `s10_reachability_probe.py`, `sports_clv_s7.py`) are correctly suppressed as guarded; `execution/fill_models.py`'s `side == "yes"` (an ORDER SIDE, not a settlement result) is correctly NOT matched, pinned by `test_acceptance_order_side_comparisons_are_not_reported`. The 6 reported scripts were deliberately left UNEDITED — they are frozen, verdicted historical probes and changing them would alter recorded results; that is exactly why this layer is advisory, not gating. **Reconciliation with L106 (not overturned):** L106 (2026-07-19) recorded L52 as "honestly terminal (test-pinned at L92), SKIPPED, recorded so the next idle run does not re-attempt it." That verdict was scoped to a **GATING** scanner, and it remains CORRECT — a gating check would false-gate ~9 frozen legacy scripts and an allowlist of them all would be the L30/L19 dishonest-theater anti-pattern. Nothing built here contradicts it: what shipped is the NON-gating advisory form (the L109/L118/L126/L144/L150/L152/L156/L157/L160/L161 pattern), which needs no allowlist and cannot false-gate — the 6 legacy sites are reported honestly to stderr and the exit code is untouched. **Nuance on L92 (row not edited):** L92 claimed `collection/settlement_ledger.py` closed this row's shared-helper gap. It closed the **WRITE path** — its scalar filter is inline inside `run()` (~L249-257) and `migrate_caches()` (~L381-385) against its own PRIVATE local `BINARY_RESULTS = ("yes","no")` (~L86), so a downstream probe joining settlement data cannot import a classifier from it. `core/settlement.py` is the **READ path** helper that did not exist. Consequence worth stating plainly: `tape/settlement_ledger/` is 100% binary (**10,605/10,605**, 2 files, `{no: 8235, yes: 2370}` — command in `findings/2026-07-25-l52-settlement-binary-result-enforcement.md`) precisely BECAUSE the collector pre-filters scalars upstream; a reader who samples that tape and concludes "Kalshi settlement is always binary" is generalizing a COLLECTOR ARTIFACT to the API, where the observed rate is 8/458 (~1.7%). The acceptance test asserts `kept_fraction == 1.0` as "the pre-filter still holds", NOT "the world is clean". **Known blind spots (both stated in the warning text, both regression-pinned as misses):** (a) the settlement token on an EARLIER line than the comparison — `scripts/probe_ladder_coherence.py:140` (`if res == "yes":`) is a genuine unguarded settlement read this line-scoped rule does NOT report; (b) file-level guard granularity — one guard anywhere in a file exempts every other hand-rolled comparison in it. A 6-site count is PRECISION evidence, not RECALL (L155). |

| L57 | 2026-07-14 | A burst-resolution cross-venue lead-lag correlation can be DOMINATED BY A SINGLE SHOCK TICK: on the June-CPI burst tape, Kalshi<->Polymarket rho(polymarket-leads) on the two CPI-sensitive July fed buckets was 0.902/0.777, but removing the single 12:30:13Z release-instant capture collapsed it to noise (0.196/0.037) AND the residual sign is unstable (flips toward Kalshi on leave-one-out) — the "lead" was that one snapshot expressed as a Pearson rho over ~100 near-flat steps, not a persistent relationship. Two corollaries confirmed this run: (i) the WIDTH x DURATION cross cleanly discriminates a real shock dislocation from a stale-quote nominal basis and generalizes L31 from single-venue wings to CROSS-VENUE dislocations — the two largest gaps ($0.079/$0.06 net of both fees) were single-capture release-instant transients while every durable gap was small ($0.01-0.04), the S6/L31 signature; (ii) `tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/` is SIZE-BLIND (only best_ask/best_bid, no depth) and `captured_at` is a single pass-level timestamp shared by all records in a capture, so it can neither prove venue simultaneity nor claim fillability — every cross-venue dislocation over it is a price observation, never a fill. | `findings/2026-07-14-s17-burst-cpi-q19.md` (Q19 CPI-burst per-event, verifier CONFIRMED-numbers / REFUTED-both-tradeable-claims) | **protocol** — a per-probe burst-window methodology gate (report leave-one-out for any single-shock lead-lag; the width x duration discriminator; the size-blind / single-pass-timestamp fillability caveat), same class as L27/L28/L32/L48 (the shock-tick and population choices are per-design, not statically assertable); cross-references L8 (lagged confound), L13 (startup artifact), L31 (nominal != capturable, now cross-venue), L39/L48 (a proxy orients but never substitutes for a size/queue-aware fill test). | L59 | 2026-07-14 | **Reversal *frequency* and reversal *magnitude* can disagree in sign of implication — a momentum/reversal precheck must report BOTH, not pick one.** S24's raw continuation frequency was 0.454 (a slight MAJORITY of jumps kept going, which alone would read as momentum), yet the sign-conditioned mean next-step pointed the opposite way (post-jump-up mean −$0.0061, post-jump-down mean +$0.0087) — a minority of large reversals carried the mean. Classifying on frequency alone would have mislabeled this DEAD-by-momentum and skipped the real (round-trip-cost) kill entirely. | `findings/2026-07-14-nearclose-fade-s24-verdict.md` (Q28/S24 Gate 2 direction precheck, verifier-CONFIRMED) | **UNENFORCED** — a per-probe verdict-methodology gate for any future momentum/reversal precheck (report reversal fraction AND sign-conditioned mean as two separate numbers before classifying); not statically assertable (population/classification choice is per-design, L6-class) and not yet encoded as an importable helper — candidate for a future `core/`-write pass alongside L27/L28/L32's dual-cut helpers. |
| L60 | 2026-07-14 | **The S6/L32 frozen-vs-movement maker dual-cut does not apply to a taker round-trip — it has a different boundary.** L32 exists because a maker's resting order can go UNFILLED when the book is frozen (no fill, no loss); but a TAKER round-trip fills both legs by crossing the spread regardless of whether the book was "frozen" at entry, so a frozen exit is a real (losing) fill, not a no-fill — bucketing taker trades by frozen-vs-movement would misapply L32's rationale. The correct place for a frozen guard on a taker jump-detection probe is at DETECTION (a zero-Δmid pair is never counted as a jump event in the first place), not at the fill-outcome layer. Records the boundary of where L32 applies (maker resting fills) vs where it doesn't (taker round-trips). | `findings/2026-07-14-nearclose-fade-s24-verdict.md` (Q28/S24 design note, verifier-CONFIRMED) | **ledger-only** — a scope clarification for L32, not a new mechanism; no code change (S24's probe already applies the frozen guard correctly at detection, per its own design section). Recorded so a future taker-round-trip probe doesn't reach for L32's dual-cut by reflex. |
| L61 | 2026-07-15 | **A scheduled-macro Kalshi ladder settles AT the print, so its resolving-month event has no post-release live quotes at all — the tradeable window ends *before* the outcome becomes public.** On the June-CPI burst tape (`tape/econ_prints/dt=2026-07-14.jsonl`) the resolving events `KXCPI-26JUN`/`KXCPICORE-26JUN`/`KXCPIYOY-26JUN` have `close_time` 12:25:00Z/12:29:00Z — *ahead* of the 12:30Z release — and vanish from the tape at 12:28:59Z; there are **zero** post-12:30Z records of the resolving ladder. The 100 "post-print" records in the 12:30–12:49Z window are all FORWARD months (`-26JUL`/`-26AUG`/…), a *different object* whose outcome the June print does not determine, and they carry ~0.88 median yes-spreads. Any "post-print staleness pickoff" (buy the now-certain bucket) or "print-overshoot fade" thesis on econ tape is therefore structurally DOA — Kalshi closes the market early precisely to deny the stale-order pickoff. Verify the resolving event's `close_time` against the release instant before proposing any post-print econ trade. | Q21 idea-gen round 2026-07-15, verifier KILL of S25 & S27 (`findings/2026-07-15-q21-idea-gen-round.md`) | **ledger-only** — a market-microstructure fact (close-before-release) + a pre-probe design check (compare resolving `close_time` to the release timestamp); per-design, not statically assertable. | L62 | 2026-07-15 | **A cross-venue ask-to-ask "gap" is not an edge when one venue's own bid-ask spread exceeds the gap.** Scoring the S26 entry condition `\|kalshi_yes_ask − poly_best_ask\| > overround_share + fee` on `tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/dt=2026-07-14.jsonl` cleared for 19.8% of records — but on these thin far-dated KXFEDDECISION buckets the Kalshi yes bid-ask spread is median 9¢ (mean 11¢, up to 30¢) and the Polymarket anchor sits **INSIDE** Kalshi's own bid-ask in **62.6%** of the entry-met records. So the "divergence" is Kalshi's half-spread against Poly's tight top-of-book ask — Kalshi's true mid already agrees with Poly, and there is nothing to converge (the size-blind top-of-book illusion, one layer worse than L39). Any Kalshi↔Polymarket divergence claim must first check whether the anchor falls inside the Kalshi bid-ask, and must name a realizable exit (round-trip the Kalshi spread vs hold-to-settlement) before calling a gap an edge. | Q21 idea-gen round 2026-07-15, verifier KILL of S26 (`findings/2026-07-15-q21-idea-gen-round.md`) | **ledger-only** — generalizes L31 (stale-nominal quote) / L39 (size-blind proxy) to the cross-venue case; a per-probe methodology gate (compute the anchor-inside-spread fraction first), same class as L6's bootstrap-unit choice. |
| L63 | 2026-07-15 | **A cross-venue laggard trade is market-neutral only if BOTH legs are held; a single-venue "take the laggard toward the anchor" is a directional bet, not an arbitrage.** S26 tried to escape S17's cross-venue rail-risk caveat by executing only the Kalshi leg (Polymarket as a free anchor, never held) — but dropping the hedge leg removes the *hedge*, not the *risk*: "convergence-hold" on a single macro bucket to its FOMC/CPI resolution is unbounded directional event risk (the S2/S16 family), and it re-inherits the exact "Kalshi is the laggard" premise that S17-burst already REFUTED as sign-unstable (residual lead flips toward Kalshi on leave-one-out). Escaping S9's data-adequacy death and S17's rail caveat only re-lands the idea on the directional-macro-bet objection. Before proposing any "single-venue convergence" trade, state whether "convergence" is defined anywhere but at settlement — if not, it is a directional position. | Q21 idea-gen round 2026-07-15, verifier KILL of S26 (`findings/2026-07-15-q21-idea-gen-round.md`) | **ledger-only** — a proposal-stage discipline (name the non-settlement convergence event or admit it is directional); cross-references S2/S16 (directional macro), S9/S17 (refuted laggard premise). |

Capture happens at the top of book, so the fillable number is the 10-contract one, not the 4,600 total — the wide spread is an **L31 wing with a two-sided tail**, not a competition gap. Always split ladder depth into "size within ~5¢ of top" vs "lottery tail" before quoting a two-sided-liquidity claim. The concrete verifier stress-check (payout=0 counterfactual) is the honest enforcement pattern to reuse, but it lives in each probe's verifier pass, not a reusable assert. |

Near-money sum-of-yes-ask runs a median 1.120 for BTC (+12¢, worse than S1's +9.84¢ overround). So the literature thesis (retail lottery demand overprices the tails → fillable near-money left cheap; Bollen & Whaley 2004 / Coval & Shumway 2001, `kb/quant-finance/net-buying-pressure-implied-distribution.md`) does NOT hold here: the overround is spread ACROSS the near-money region, not concentrated in the wings, and the directional skew that does exist is one-sided (upside rich, downside fair) and lives inside the spread (maker-only, cross-refs L26/L31). **Any future held-to-settlement single-near-money-leg crypto candidate is presumptively S1-on-crypto and must clear a win-rate-above-ask-plus-fee calibration cut before registration.** | 2026-07-16 Q21 round, S35/S36 verifier kills; `findings/2026-07-16-q21-idea-gen-round.md` | **ledger-only** — an empirical venue fact plus a per-design pre-probe calibration check; not statically assertable (the win-rate-vs-ask calibration is a per-probe numeric gate, same class as L28, and the venue-shape fact is an observation like L12/L26/L31). Cross-refs L1/L12/L54/L58. |
| L88 | 2026-07-16 | **A Kalshi `crypto_hourly` "between" range-ladder has NO fillable ATM up/down directional instrument.** It lists ~186 identical fixed-width ($100) brackets plus two tail caps (`less`/`greater`); a single near-ATM bracket is a pin/volatility bet — "closes in [floor,cap)", non-monotonic in direction — that does NOT express P(up). To bet direction you must buy the STRIP of all brackets above spot, which inherits the ladder's ~189% bracket_sum overround (2.89) plus a per-leg 0.07 taker fee floor plus each leg's own half-spread. **Any directional-crypto proposal must name at proposal time whether it trades the multi-leg strip (and then price the overround + per-leg fee floor) or a single bracket (which is a pin bet, not a direction bet); absent a fillable directional instrument, kill at idea.** Sibling to L68 (name your trade/data leg at proposal time). | 2026-07-16 Q21 round, S37 verifier kill; `findings/2026-07-16-q21-idea-gen-round.md` | **ledger-only** — a structural product fact (the range-ladder simply lists no directional binary; the strip-overround is arithmetic). Not statically assertable — a scanner cannot know a product's instrument set; enforced as proposal-time discipline, cross-refs S10 graveyard / L26. |
| L89 | 2026-07-17 | A **clamp-vs-rounding discriminator (dead-band vs quantization) must test the GAP relative to the data's OWN granularity — never against an absolute threshold.** The two hypotheses for a zero-heavy series are: (a) a **dead-band clamp** — rates inside a ±band forced to *exactly* 0 while the surviving nonzeros stay **continuous/unquantized**; vs (b) a **symmetric-rounding/quantization artifact** — the "zeros" are a lattice bucket straddling zero and the nonzeros sit on that *same* tick grid. The decisive test is therefore (i) are the nonzeros continuous or lattice-quantized, and (ii) is there a **hard gap in the open interval just above zero**. Q42: the Kalshi perp finalized funding backfill (1,447 `broker_truth` prints, 2026-06-03→07-16, 13 contracts) has a pooled exact-zero fraction 0.762 with a hard gap in `(0, 1e-4)` — **1,102** exact zeros, **0** nonzeros in `(0, 1e-4)`, **186** in `[1e-4, 1.5e-4)` — and the smallest nonzero `|rate|` *varies* per contract (BTC 1.0004e-4 … SUI 1.0560e-4, a floor near ~1e-4, not one shared value). Because the nonzeros are continuous rather than on a 1e-4 lattice, the zeros are a **genuine ±1bp clamp**, not a rounding bucket — confirmed on 12/13 contracts (KXLINKPERP undecidable, 1 nonzero print). An absolute-threshold test ("call it a clamp if zeros dominate below some fixed ε") would misclassify EITHER a fine-tick series (real clamp hidden under a coarse ε) OR a coarse-tick series (rounding mistaken for a clamp). Do NOT headline an inferred single tick / ticks-from-zero figure — it is a sample-dependent proxy, not a physical venue tick. Same per-probe-methodology-gate family as L6 (bootstrap the right unit) / L27 (magnitude-vs-tick) / L28 (observability precheck). | `findings/2026-07-17-q42-funding-clamp-characterization.md` (Q42 part 1, verifier-CONFIRMED); `scripts/q42_funding_clamp_probe.py` | **test (this discriminator) + protocol (the general principle)** — the gap-based discriminator is pinned for the Q42 probe by `tests/test_q42_funding_clamp_probe.py::test_clamp_signature_clear_gap` and `::test_rounding_signature_one_tick_from_zero` (clear-gap vs one-tick-from-zero fixtures) plus `::test_infer_tick_smallest_gap`. The GENERAL methodology (test the gap relative to the data's own granularity, never an absolute threshold) is a per-probe verdict-methodology judgment — like L6/L27/L28 the population and granularity are per-design, so it is **not statically assertable** at the invariants layer (the scanner scans text, it cannot evaluate a series' lattice structure) and is terminal as **protocol** once encoded in probe precedents. NOTE — a second Q42 lesson candidate (route population/sample stdev through `core.stats.safe_pstdev`, which the probe does at `scripts/q42_funding_clamp_probe.py:172` with an `n>=4` guard) was NOT given its own row: it is already covered at the `invariant` end of the gradient by **Hard Rule #2 / L2** (`inv_no_bare_pstdev` + `inv_no_pstdev_import` in `scripts/invariants.py`, sanctioned to `core/stats.py`); the probe simply complied, so a new row would restate an enforced rule rather than move any lesson along the gradient. |
| L90 | 2026-07-17 | Kalshi's settled `/markets` objects carry numeric settlement fields under **suffixed** names, not the bare ones a naive reader would reach for: `settlement_value_dollars`, `volume_fp`, `open_interest_fp` (all wire-format strings, sometimes blank). A collector that reads `settlement_value`/`volume`/`open_interest` directly silently gets `None` for every row instead of erroring — a completeness-invalidating bug that looks like clean output. `collection/settlement_ledger.py` parses the `_dollars`/`_fp` variants explicitly and treats a blank/unparseable string as an honest `None`, never a fabricated `0`. | Q45 build (`collection/settlement_ledger.py:143-146`, `_to_float`); `findings/`-class discovery, no dedicated finding filed (collector build) | **test** — pinned by `tests/test_settlement_ledger.py`'s field-parsing fixtures; UNENFORCED as a general static invariant (no shared "read Kalshi's `_fp`/`_dollars` suffix convention" helper exists yet outside this module to anchor a scanner rule to). |
| L91 | 2026-07-17 | `/events?status=settled&with_nested_markets=true` returns **empty nested `markets` arrays platform-wide** on Kalshi's public API — the per-market settlement fields (`result`, `settlement_value_dollars`, `volume_fp`, `open_interest_fp`) live only on the `/markets` object, not the event. A systematic settled-market harvest that follows the events-endpoint route literally would need one extra `/markets?event_ticker=...` call per event (~1.7k calls/day at Q45's discovery volume) — exactly the unbounded-pull expansion L10 warns against. The bounded alternative is to paginate `/markets?status=settled` directly (the same endpoint the four legacy `qNN_settlement_cache` probes already called, just without a series filter) — one capped, cursor-paginated sweep returns every field needed in place. | Q45 build (`collection/settlement_ledger.py` module docstring, `fetch_settled_markets`); live probe against the real Kalshi API this run | **protocol** — an API-shape discovery (which endpoint actually carries the fields, not a code bug); not statically assertable (a scanner cannot know what a live endpoint returns). | L96 | 2026-07-18 | **Before proposing (or building) any `tape/universe_sweep/` strategy that differences a market across sweeps or joins its entry-BBO depth to settlement, verify the tape actually supports a per-market panel — schema presence ≠ signal presence, and a cross-section ≠ a time series.** Two failures found the same run (S39/S38 idea-stage kills): (a) `volume_24h` is persisted **0.0 on 100% of the 100,000 committed census rows** (the field exists, `volume`/`open_interest` are populated, but the specific signal is identically zero — likely a `volume_24h_fp` source-field-name bug in `collection/universe_sweep.py`), so a "volume-surge" trigger is uncomputable; (b) the 20k-call-cap (`PAGE_LIMIT=1000 × MAX_CALLS=20`) truncates the >80k-market cursor mid-stream, so each pass paginates a **disjoint slice — 0 ticker overlap across all 5 committed passes** — meaning `universe_sweep` is a bag of one-shot cross-sections, NOT a re-observation of the same markets. Any consecutive-sweep delta is structurally impossible, and per-market entry-depth→settlement joins get only the trickle of tickers that recur by luck. Check `volume_24h!=0` count AND cross-capture ticker-overlap > 0 on the real tape before the mechanism is even conceivable. Same "attack the data claim, don't trust the proposal" family as L9/L43/L50 (join reality) and the general L68 (name+verify your data leg at proposal time). | 2026-07-18 Q21 round, S38+S39 verifier kills; `findings/2026-07-18-q21-idea-gen-round.md` | **ledger-only + collector-bug candidate** — the disjoint-slice property is arithmetic (cap < universe) and the `volume_24h=0` is a concrete `collection/universe_sweep.py` field-mapping bug worth a fix in a future collector run (PR #107 already has Ryan on the universe_sweep cap/storage design calls). Not statically assertable as a strategy rule (a scanner cannot know a probe assumes a panel); enforced as proposal-time data-reality discipline. |
| L97 | 2026-07-18 | **A Kalshi incentive-program parameter (e.g. `discount_factor_bps=5000` from `/incentive_programs`) is `synthetic` for economic modeling until its mechanism is pinned by a real fee-paid-on-a-fill check or vendor confirmation — never treat a 4-digit bps field as a settled fee/rebate fact.** The Q37 probe (`scripts/weather_fee_schedule_probe.py`) found the *actual* weather fee schedule is standard (`fee_type=quadratic`, `fee_multiplier=1`, empty `/series`+`/events/fee_changes`); the "50%" lives ONLY inside a separate `incentive_programs` object whose payout mechanics (`discount_factor_bps` vs the separate `period_reward` centi-cent pool) `kb/kalshi-api/03-fees-and-breakeven.md` explicitly flags as undocumented beyond field names. A Liquidity Incentive Program is structurally a reward *pool*, so the parameter is at least as likely a reward weight as a maker-fee discount. Reading it as "5000 bps → 50% off my fill's fee" and sizing a strategy on it is the pt1 plausible-unattacked-number failure mode (CLAUDE.md prime directive #1). Any candidate whose viability rests on such a parameter must carry a `mechanic_unverified` block on graduation, not a `broker_truth` fee — and idea-stage proposals citing it as "confirmed-live" overstate the source. | 2026-07-18 Q21 round, S40 verifier kill; `kb/kalshi-api/03-fees-and-breakeven.md`, `findings/2026-07-18-q21-idea-gen-round.md` | **ledger-only** — a trust/provenance discipline (the field value is real; its economic *meaning* is unpinned). Not statically assertable (a scanner cannot know a number's real-world mechanic); enforced as proposal-time discipline, cross-refs the Hard-Rule-#1 real-ask/source-tag bar and the 2026-06-18 "LIP maker-rebate harvest" dead-end ledger. |

| L110 | 2026-07-19 | Supersedes L109's enforcement column (lesson content unchanged: L25's shape-check flags a directory-shaped `dt=<date>` entry as wrong-shape but never says what to DO about one). Built `scripts/invariants.py::_tape_dir_shape_orphan_classification()`/`tape_dir_shape_orphan_warning()` (same non-gating stderr-advisory pattern as L25/L75) that classifies every directory-shaped `dt=<date>` entry for GC dispatch: **"superseded"** when a canonical `dt=<date>.jsonl` for the SAME date already coexists (pure post-fix debris, safe to delete) vs **"unrecoverable"** when no canonical file exists for that date AND the family has since captured a LATER canonical day (forward collection has moved on — this day can never self-heal via normal cadence, a human must decide backfill-or-accept-the-gap). A directory at/after the family's newest canonical day is deliberately left unclassified — collection may still be mid-write, so flagging it for GC would be premature. Compounding kill: `orderbook_depth` has no `volume`/`trade`/`last_price` field, so a maker fill is unobservable at hourly cadence — any fill-sim would synthesize the fill (`synthetic`-as-fill-price, prime-directive-forbidden, the pt1 failure mode). | L148 | 2026-07-24 | **Generalizes L142** (the conflict-marker-only committed-tape gate) into a full JSON-per-line validity gate — BUT scope matters more than leniency. Extending L142's check to "every line of every committed tape `.jsonl` must parse as JSON" is correct, yet a raw `rglob` filesystem walk also scans UNTRACKED/in-flight files, so a collector mid-append (a torn final line, no trailing newline) would false-fail a GATING invariant and wedge the autonomous loop on data that was never committed. The fix is SCOPE, not torn-line tolerance: restrict the scan to git-TRACKED tape only (`git ls-files -- tape`, filtered to `.jsonl`), with an offline-safe empty-set fallback (any git failure → skip, never wedge). A committed torn/truncated line IS real corruption and must STILL be caught — leniency there would defeat the purpose. The new gate explicitly does NOT double-report L142's own conflict-marker lines (those keep L142's dedicated diagnostic). | L154 | 2026-07-25 | **A float-size contract that is only DOCUMENTED gets violated by the very lane that most depends on it: the paper fill model.** L47's rule ("never coerce a ladder level's size to int without an explicit, justified rounding rule") was written into `collection/normalize.py::normalize_snapshot`'s docstring at the shared normalizer — and yet `execution/fill_models.py::_taker_depth`, the code path that produces every `taker_depth` paper fill, carried a bare `take = min(remaining, int(size))` with no stated rounding rule, no L47 citation, and no test pinning the intent. The violation is REAL but numerically BENIGN and I am not inflating it: `int(size)` on a non-negative float IS a floor, which is the conservative direction (it under-states liftable depth, never manufactures it), the real tape carries no negative or NaN sizes, and a full replay of real committed ladders through the old and new code produced byte-identical fills — **zero paper P&L moved**. The residual exposure was sub-contract per level (max 0.999 contracts of unlifted depth) plus the latent crash/negative-depth path on a malformed size. The generalizable point is about ENFORCEMENT PLACEMENT, not about this one truncation: a docstring at the producer (`collection/`) does not bind a consumer in another lane (`execution/`), and a rule with no single callable home gets re-implemented inline at every consumer — the same re-derive-per-site pattern L33/L35/L36/L49 closed for the bootstrap, magnitude-gate, floor-precheck and close-time helpers. Real-tape census supporting L47's premise, re-derived independently this run over all 17 files of `tape/orderbook_depth/dt=*.jsonl` (`yes_bids`+`no_bids` levels; these are DESCRIPTIVE level COUNTS, not prices — the ladders come from records tagged `price_source_tags={"asks":"real_ask","bids":"real_bid"}`): **14,756,132 levels · 747,412 fractional (5.07%) · 5,832 with 0 < size < 1 · 0 zero-size · 0 negative · max 13,209,380.2**. An audit of every int-coercion site across `execution/`, `core/`, `collection/`, `scripts/`, `analysis/`, `validation/` found this to be the ONLY one — the queue-aware fill-sims (S14/S19/Q24/Q27/Q30) already keep queue-ahead depth as a float (`queue_ahead_at`, `bid_size_at_or_above`, `core.depth.capturable_depth` all return `float` and compare with a `- 1e-9` tolerance), so **no published verdict carries an optimistic queue-truncation bias**. Notably the exact shape `_taker_depth` would most plausibly regress into via a copy from `analysis/observatory/features.py:160` (`int(no_bids[0][1])`) IS covered, but e.g. a renamed intermediate or a multi-line call is not; the hard pin against a P&L-moving regression is the real-tape replay test, not the advisory. The broader placement lesson ("a producer-side docstring does not bind a consumer in another lane; give a rule one callable home") is **protocol** — not statically assertable in general, but now instantiated for this rule. |
| L155 | 2026-07-25 | **A lexical advisory reporting 0 issues on a clean tree is evidence of PRECISION only, never of RECALL.** "0 hits" answers "does this rule fire on correct code?" and says nothing about "would it fire on the bug?" — recall can only be established by a CONSTRUCTED-NEGATIVE corpus: a set of realistic violation shapes asserted to FIRE, checked in alongside the rule. It follows that an enforcement's ledger row must state the **tested** shape set, not the intent; otherwise the kb records coverage the code does not have, and a future run reads the row, believes the class is guarded, and stops looking. Instance from this run: L47's new advisory (`scripts/invariants.py::_ladder_size_coercion_issues`) reported 0 sites on the clean tree and its L47 row claimed it "flags any NEW `int()`/`round()`/`floor()`/`ceil()` on a size-ish name". A verifier probe of **15 realistic shapes refuted that in 10 of the 13 genuine-violation cases** — including `int(no_bids[0][1])`, the double-subscript form `analysis/observatory/features.py:160-161` ALREADY writes for `touch_queue` (i.e. the single likeliest reintroduction path was invisible), plus `int(float(size))`, `math.trunc(size)`, bare `int(depth)`, `int(row[1])`, renamed intermediates, size-ish prefixes, multi-line calls, `size // 1` and `'%d' % size`. This **generalizes L151** ("when a hazard is conditional on a runtime the test environment does not exercise, a passing suite is not evidence") from runtime-conditional hazards to LEXICAL-PROXY coverage claims: L151's suite was green because the hazardous input was never presented; this advisory was green because the hazardous SHAPE was never presented. Same failure mode, different axis — absence of a signal from an instrument nobody probed is not evidence of absence. Corollary, applied here: where widening a lexical rule to catch a shape would false-positive on legitimate code (`depth` is an already-integer level COUNT in the orderbook_depth schema; `row`/`pair`/`entry` are ordinary iteration names), the honest terminal state is a DOCUMENTED, regression-tested recall hole, not a guessed-at wider regex. | this run (2026-07-25 idle-run enforcement pass, verifier attack on the L47 milestone); the verifier's 15-shape adversarial probe (13 genuine-violation shapes, 10 missed pre-fix, 3 widened post-fix, 7 recorded as deliberate holes); L47 and L154 enforcement columns corrected in the same pass; generalizes L151 | **test (a constructed-negative corpus, the enforcement this row prescribes) + protocol** — `tests/test_invariants.py::test_ladder_size_coercion_fires_on_tested_shape_set` (15 parametrized shapes asserted to FIRE) and `::test_ladder_size_coercion_known_blind_spots_are_misses` (12 parametrized shapes asserted to MISS, so the documented hole is itself regression-tested and a future widening must delete an entry ON PURPOSE). The matcher was widened for the three shapes where precision was safe (multi-subscript `no_bids[0][1]`/`ladder[i][1]`, nested cast `int(float(size))`, `math.trunc`) while holding 0 issues on the real tree; the rest are recorded as holes in code comments, in `ladder_size_coercion_warning`'s message text, and in L47's enforcement column. The GENERAL rule ("every new lexical/static advisory ships with a fires-on corpus, and its ledger row states the tested shape set") is **protocol**, not itself statically assertable — no regex can tell whether an arbitrary advisory's test file constitutes an adequate adversarial corpus; that is a reviewer/verifier judgment, which is exactly what caught this one. Addressed to any future run adding a rule to `scripts/invariants.py`. |

So the failure is not "the alarm rang into an empty room"; it is "the alarm was never wired to a clock." The generalizable rule: a monitoring capability only counts as enforcement once it is attached to **the one command every run is REQUIRED to run and to read** — in this repo that is `scripts/invariants.py --full` (LOOP-QUEUE step 4), not a standalone script whose invocation depends on somebody remembering. Corollary for severity-vs-gating: attaching a check to the gate command does NOT require making it gating; a dead VPS cron is severe AND physically un-fixable from a cloud sandbox, so gating on it would convert one silent failure into an indefinite loop halt. Then it died again, and stayed dead 61.7h unnoticed. **Rule: no collector-recovery finding may be filed without >=24 consecutive hours of the expected signature bucket observed post-restart, and the finding MUST state the dwell it actually observed.** A recovery claim with no dwell number is a hypothesis wearing a verdict's clothes — and it is worse than no claim, because it retires the queue's attention on the outage. | `findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md`; supersedes the confidence (not the observations) of L129 / `findings/2026-07-22-vps-collector-recovered-post-pr151.md` | **test + non-gating advisory (BUILT 2026-07-25, idle-run policy (a); lesson text unchanged, stale `UNENFORCED` marker corrected by the kb-distiller convention)** — `scripts/invariants.py::_recovery_dwell_issues`/`recovery_dwell_warning`, wired into `main()`'s `--full` stderr-only path (never appended to the gating `failures` list; every failure mode — detector exception, non-str formatter return, `BaseException`/`SystemExit` from the formatter — is pinned to leave the exit code untouched). Headline-scoped lexical proxy per L155's own discipline: recovery-class membership reads the filename slug + first `# ` H1 only (a body-wide match would be a ~16x precision disaster on the 2026-07-25 tree — 16/83 findings mention "recover" in the body, only 1 in its headline), and the advisory states its TESTED coverage rather than its intent. `tests/test_recovery_dwell_advisory.py` (48 tests): a constructed-positive corpus (must fire), a constructed-negative corpus incl. the L162-style narrow supersession escape hatch, a constructed-blind-spot corpus of genuine misses asserted as misses (repair-verb headlines, H2-only claims, outage-duration-read-as-dwell — measured recall 18/22 on the full adversarial corpus, honestly documented rather than silently widened), and a HARD acceptance suite over the real `findings/` tree. | L158 | 2026-07-25 | **Commit-author archaeology is UNSOUND on a squash-merge repo — collector-liveness must be derived from tape content, never from authorship.** `git log HEAD --author=vps-collector` returns **ZERO** commits, despite ~330 such commits genuinely existing on side refs; `main` squash-merges, so the author identity is erased on the mainline, and the newest VPS commit is unreachable from any ref in a fresh clone. Any liveness/attribution claim built on `git log --author`, commit messages, or committer timestamps will therefore read a live collector as dead (or, on a differently-shaped ref set, a dead one as live) purely as an artifact of merge policy. The sound method is per-line `captured_at` **minute-of-hour bucketing** (vps = minutes 20-29, cloud = minutes 50-59) restricted to `kind=="hourly-dual"` families, per `scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py`'s `COLLECTOR_MINUTE_BUCKETS` — tape content is the durable artifact; git topology is not. Same durability principle applies to citing WHERE a tape line came from: cite its `captured_at` (and the UTC-hour gate it implies), never "the commit that added it" — that answer varies by ref set. | `findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md`; verifier fetched all 487 remote heads to establish the zero-reachable-commit fact; extends L118's minute-bucket calibration | **invariant (enforced by construction) + test** — the dead-collector-leg advisory shipped this run in `scripts/invariants.py` derives liveness **exclusively** from committed-tape `captured_at` minute buckets: it makes no network call, runs no git command, and reads no commit-author string. The unsound method is therefore not merely discouraged but absent from the enforcement path, and `tests/test_dead_collector_leg_advisory.py` exercises it over real tape. |
| L159 | 2026-07-25 | **The 09-UTC econ slot is a PERMANENT false `:2x` liveness signal — a raw minute-bucket count will report a dead VPS leg as alive once a day, forever.** `econ_prints`/`anomalies` land at **09:20-09:29** (an earlier draft of this row narrowed the span to 09:25-09:29; the true observed span inside the bucket is 09:20-09:29 — e.g. `econ_prints` dt=2026-07-19 at 09:20 and 09:21, dt=2026-07-21 at 09:23/09:24/09:28/09:29), squarely inside the VPS `:20-29` bucket, so any raw `:20-29` line count forges a live-VPS reading. This is not hypothetical drift: the artifact was present through the ENTIRE first outage (07-19T09, 07-20T09, 07-21T09 — i.e. on every single day the leg was confirmed dead) and again on 07-23T09 during the second. The fix is to restrict liveness counts to `kind=="hourly-dual"` families, which are the only ones actually written by both staggered legs. Generalizes past the econ case: a minute-of-hour signature is only an identity for legs that share the same schedule shape; mixing a differently-scheduled family into the count destroys the signature. | `findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md`; refines L118 (`collector_bucket()`) and L128; the 4 contaminating slot-days named above are re-derived from committed tape | **invariant (enforced by construction) + test** — the advisory shipped this run restricts its liveness computation to `kind=="hourly-dual"` families (config imported from `scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py`, not re-declared), so the artifact cannot reach its verdict; pinned by `tests/test_dead_collector_leg_advisory.py`. **Standing caveat, recorded honestly:** `scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py`'s own RAW per-bucket counts still carry the artifact — its `collector_diagnosis` is unaffected, but a human reading its raw `:20-29` numbers can still be fooled, and that half is **not** fixed by this run. |
| L160 | 2026-07-25 | **Tree-hash equality is the cheap, authoritative stranded-tape containment test; `git merge-base --is-ancestor` must NEVER be used for it.** `git rev-parse <branch>:tape` vs `git rev-parse HEAD:tape` settles "is any tape line stranded on this branch?" in ONE command with no per-file work (this run: `origin/tape/hourly-202607250406Z:tape` == `HEAD:tape` == `23b44416b723b76fca0b130feba53f719d2b5676` → zero missing lines, sweep complete). Per-file `comm` is only needed AFTER a hash mismatch, to localize WHICH lines differ. The anti-pattern is specific and costly: because `main` **squash-merges**, a tape branch is *never* an ancestor of `HEAD` even when its content is fully contained — so `git merge-base --is-ancestor` produces a false "stranded" signal **every single time**, sending step-0b sweeps chasing tape that is already merged. (Same root cause as L158: squash-merge destroys topology-based reasoning; content-based reasoning survives it.) | step-0b stranded-tape sweep by a separate `tape-auditor`, 2026-07-25 run; `findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md` (§3 + "Reproduce"); relates to L158 | **tool (enforced) + test** — built this run as `scripts/tape_branch_sweep.py`, with a correction this row's own framing needed: on an APPEND-ONLY tree a tree-hash MISMATCH does NOT by itself mean "not contained" — `tape/` grows every hour, so any branch older than the single most-recently-diverged one will mismatch HEAD's tree even when every line it carries is fully present in HEAD's now-larger files (this row's own worked example only held because it checked the newest branch immediately). A mismatch now falls back to a per-file LINE-SET check (`per_file_containment`), scoped via `diff_changed_files`/`--diff-filter=AM` between the two tree objects rather than a full tree walk — a branch's `tape/` snapshot is a full historical snapshot (13,644 files measured live), of which a typical hourly-pass branch touches a handful; enumerating the whole tree per branch made an early draft of this tool time out on 20 branches, the diff-scoped version swept the real 192-branch backlog in ~30s. No call to `git merge-base --is-ancestor` anywhere in the module — pinned by a source-text test (`tests/test_tape_branch_sweep.py::TestNeverUsesMergeBaseIsAncestor`, code-minus-docstring scan via `ast`). 31 offline tests, real temporary git repos (not mocked subprocess), including a fixture pinned to the exact "old snapshot, hash-mismatched, still contained" case. **Live sweep of all 192 real `tape/*` branches (2026-07-25):** 12 fully verified contained, 167 no-problem-found-but-unverified (bulk-family files, e.g. `orderbook_depth`/`universe_sweep`/`sports_pairs`, size-guard-skipped at 2MB rather than read in full — flagged, never silently trusted), 13 flagged "missing" that on inspection are ALL benign (8 are wording differences in the prose doc `tape/cloud-env-check.md`, not append-only tape; 5 are pre-existing git conflict-marker artifacts, L142's own bug class, already correctly excluded from HEAD) — **zero genuine stranded tape found across the entire historical backlog.** See `findings/2026-07-25-tape-branch-sweep-tool-and-backlog-audit.md`. |
| L161 | 2026-07-25 | **Malformed tape-branch names are a RECURRING defect, not a one-off — and a naive lexical "newest branch" sweep silently mis-triages them, which is a data-loss risk in step-0b.** **44 of 192** remote `tape/*` branches fail the canonical name regex `^tape/hourly-[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{4}Z$`, measured 2026-07-25 ~08:5xZ by the exact command this row ships (an earlier draft of this row said "roughly 13-20 of 192" with no command and no definition of "malformed" — unfalsifiable, and re-derived to 44-45 of 192-193 minutes later; an independent verifier measured 45 of 193 in the same hour, i.e. **the count DRIFTS as branches accumulate**, which is precisely why a row asserting a count over refs must inline its command rather than freeze a number): `git ls-remote --heads origin 'refs/heads/tape/*' | awk '{print $2}' | sed 's|refs/heads/||' > /tmp/br.txt` then `wc -l < /tmp/br.txt` (192 total), `grep -cE '^tape/hourly-[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{4}Z$' /tmp/br.txt` (148 well-formed), `grep -vcE '^tape/hourly-[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{4}Z$' /tmp/br.txt` (44 failures). The dominant malformed shape is ~22 branches of the form `hourly-YYYYMMDDHHMMZ` — the `T` separator missing — including this run's own newest branch `tape/hourly-202607250406Z`. **The substance is unchanged and still verified:** `tape/hourly-Z` and `tape/hourly-` (no timestamp at all) both exist. Because `Z` and the empty suffix sort AFTER every dated `tape/hourly-*` branch lexically, a sweep that picks "the newest-looking name" picks one of these degenerate branches, concludes it has already swept past everything, and leaves real unswept tape behind — silently. **Rule: any branch failing the name regex must be triaged by COMMIT DATE, never by name order**, and the count of name-regex failures should be reported by the sweep rather than skipped quietly. | step-0b stranded-tape sweep by a separate `tape-auditor`, 2026-07-25 run; `findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md` (§3) | **tool (enforced) + test** — the same `scripts/tape_branch_sweep.py` built for L160 implements this: `is_malformed_branch_name` classifies every branch against the canonical regex, and for malformed names `triage_branch` resolves `commit_date` via `git log -1 --format=%cI` (never name order); `format_report`'s malformed section is sorted by that date. The malformed-branch count is always printed, never skipped quietly. Live run (2026-07-25) found **44 of 192** malformed — matching this row's own measurement exactly. Pinned by `tests/test_tape_branch_sweep.py::TestNameValidation` (7 cases incl. the exact `hourly-Z`/`hourly-`/missing-`T` shapes this row names) and `TestFetchAndTriage` (a malformed-but-contained fixture branch asserts `commit_date is not None`). See `findings/2026-07-25-tape-branch-sweep-tool-and-backlog-audit.md`. |

| L168 | 2026-07-26 | **A 200-OK fetch is not a captured book.** `tape/orderbook_depth/`'s crypto (`KXBTC`/`KXETH`) records go completely HOLLOW (`yes_bids=[]`, `no_bids=[]`, `depth=0`, yet still tagged `real_ask`/`real_bid`) whenever the fetch reaches a ticker at or after its own close — 15,175 of 339,947 committed lines through `dt=2026-07-25` (4.46%), rising to 100% on `dt=2026-07-23`/`dt=2026-07-25` — and `completeness_ok` (computed in `collection/orderbook_depth.py`, never persisted to the tape line) cannot see it, because an empty book IS a valid HTTP 200 response. Two compounding causes, both distinct from ordinary pass-duration overrun: (1) a pass's ticker *discovery* step can run before the hour rolls, so the fetch targets an already-closed contract from the start (789 records, verified: capture `20260723T005605Z` stamped `00:56:05Z` fetched 488 records for markets closing `01:00:00Z`, i.e. *before* its own stamped time by the naive reading — see L169's sibling finding); (2) `captured_at` is a PASS-START timestamp replicated onto every record in a multi-minute capture (1 distinct value per `capture_id`, 425/425 captures verified), so a late-pass fetch's true fetch time can trail its recorded timestamp by the whole pass duration — proven on the same capture, whose 488 "00:56:05Z" records were provably fetched >=235s later, at/after their ticker's 01:00:00Z close. For any deadline-bearing market family, completeness must be measured relative to the ticker's own deadline, not merely to whether the HTTP call succeeded. | `findings/2026-07-26-orderbook-depth-hollow-crypto-ladders.md`; two-agent (tape-auditor find + independent `verifier` re-derivation, both against real tape); `scripts/orderbook_depth_hollow_ladder_audit.py` | **test (detection) + UNENFORCED (repair)** — `scripts/orderbook_depth_hollow_ladder_audit.py` (+ `tests/test_orderbook_depth_hollow_ladder_audit.py`, 2 HARD real-tape acceptance tests) is the read-only reproducer, and a non-gating `scripts/invariants.py::hollow_crypto_ladder_warning` advisory (+ `tests/test_hollow_crypto_ladder_advisory.py`) now surfaces any recent day >=50% crypto-hollow. The REPAIR (per-record fetch timestamp; deadline-first ticker ordering so crypto is fetched before non-expiring families; `run()` counting a post-close fetch as a drop) is a `collection/orderbook_depth.py` change — Ryan-gated collector lane, not built this run. |
| L169 | 2026-07-26 | **When a suspected failure mechanism is itself a function of minute-of-hour, a minute-of-hour collector-leg attribution is collinear with the mechanism and cannot be tested against it.** The first-pass `tape-auditor` finding attributed L168's hollow-ladder defect to "the cloud leg degrading" (a hand-rolled minute-bucket split gave cloud 52,477/28.9% hollow vs VPS 64,274/0.0%) — an independent `verifier` REFUTED the causal claim two ways: (a) using the project's OWN `scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::collector_bucket` instead of the hand-rolled rule changes the cloud figure to 54,055/28.0% (the hand-rolled version had silently dropped 1,578 clean off-slot records from its own denominator, inflating the rate); (b) the discriminating case is an off-schedule cloud-hour capture at minute :41 (`20260721T224112Z`, ~18.8min runway) that is 488/488 CLEAN — a cloud-leg capture with enough runway is fine, so "cloud" was never the operative variable, "runway to close" was (see L168). Rule: any collector-leg split MUST (i) use the repo's own `collector_bucket`, never a re-hand-rolled minute rule, and (ii) be checked against an off-schedule/off-slot capture in the SAME leg as a discriminator before a leg-identity causal claim ships — a same-schedule-only split can never distinguish "this leg is worse" from "this leg's slot has less of whatever the real variable is." | `findings/2026-07-26-orderbook-depth-hollow-crypto-ladders.md`; verifier review of the tape-auditor's first-pass claim, 2026-07-26 | **test (partial) + protocol** — `scripts/orderbook_depth_hollow_ladder_audit.py::audit_leg_crosstab` imports `tape_gap_monitor.collector_bucket` rather than re-deriving a minute rule (pinned by `test_leg_crosstab_uses_tape_gap_monitor_collector_bucket`), and its docstring/the finding's own write-up state the leg-crosstab table is DESCRIPTIVE ONLY, not causal. No static scanner can catch a future hand-rolled minute-split elsewhere in the tree (a lexical proxy would need to know what counts as "a leg-causal claim") — the discipline is methodological, same terminal shape as L27/L28/L69/L165. |
| L170 | 2026-07-26 | **A collector's "idempotent across staggered collectors" claim is only as global as the tape it dedups against — and this repo's tape is routinely NOT merged when a second collector runs.** `collection/hyperliquid_funding.py::_committed_time_ms` dedups a pass's "already archived" set against the LOCAL working tree only. With two collectors (VPS + cloud) racing on unmerged `tape/hourly-*`/`tape/burst-*` fallback branches (LOOP-QUEUE.md step 0b's standing state — 189 such branches on `origin` at audit time), each can independently see "nothing new" and both archive the same print. Blast radius today is nil — the only current consumer, `collect_hl_hourly`, already re-dedups on `(coin, hour_index)` before use — but the module's own docstring overclaimed unconditional global idempotency, which would mislead a future consumer that aggregates raw rows (e.g. a naive `n_prints` count) into trusting a guarantee the collector doesn't actually provide. Same failure class as any "per-print/per-line dedup against committed tape" claim in this repo's other incremental collectors — worth a grep-check the next time one is audited. | `findings/2026-07-26-hyperliquid-funding-tape-audit.md` (tape-auditor subagent + main-context fix, 2026-07-26 idle-run policy (c)) | **protocol, encoded + test** — `collection/hyperliquid_funding.py`'s docstring now states the branch-local caveat explicitly instead of the prior overclaim; `tests/test_q42_crossvenue_funding_join.py::test_collect_hl_dedups_cross_branch_duplicate_records` reproduces the exact real-tape scenario (same `(coin, time_ms)` print in two separate records) and pins that `collect_hl_hourly` handles it correctly today. The underlying tape's 2 duplicate lines are NOT rewritten (append-only) — this closes the code/doc gap, not the historical data point, which is harmless per the above. |
| L171 | 2026-07-26 | **A collector whose record carries a retrospective list (not one point-in-time observation) cannot be audited for coverage by `dt=` day-file presence — that only measures WHEN a pass ran, not what history it captured.** `tape/hyperliquid_funding/` has no committed file for `dt=2026-07-18`…`dt=2026-07-21` (the L127 VPS-freeze window), which reads as a 4-day gap by file-count — but the union of every record's `prints[].time_ms` across the whole family is 1282 distinct hourly prints per coin, 2026-06-03T00:00Z→2026-07-26T09:00Z, 0 non-1h steps: the post-freeze catch-up pass backfilled the entire window in one paginated call. The correct coverage unit for this shape is the union of the embedded historical timestamp field, not the day-file list. Same shape would apply to any future collector whose payload is "everything since X," not "what I see right now" (the `mode=backfill` records already committed elsewhere in this repo are the general case). | `findings/2026-07-26-hyperliquid-funding-tape-audit.md` (tape-auditor subagent, 2026-07-26 idle-run policy (c)) | **test** — see L172 (formal disposition, this same day). |

| L181 | 2026-07-26 | **Tightening one gate can LOOSEN another — re-derive BOTH after any change to the population.** Adding `MIN_ENTRY_EDGE` (a correct fix, L176/L179) removed the float-dust legs that had been holding the fabricated-release attack's bootstrap CI below one tick, so `clears_tick_magnitude` flipped **False → True** and the adversarial case got *stronger* on that gate while getting weaker on the one that was actually fixed. The general rule: **L27's magnitude gate filters ECONOMIC INSIGNIFICANCE, never POPULATION LEGITIMACY** — a fabricated population of large edges sails through it. Whenever two gates are cited as JOINTLY guarding an output, any change to the population that feeds them invalidates the joint claim, and the adversarial fixtures must be re-run, not assumed. | Verifier round 2, Q48/S55 prep, 2026-07-26; the concrete instance is L178's row (with its exact re-derivation command).
