| L189 | 2026-07-27 | **A detector that reports 0 issues must PUBLISH its recall, not merely have one.** `scripts/invariants.py::_stale_unenforced_candidate_issues()` — built as L152's own follow-up, with the single job of catching lesson rows still marked `**UNENFORCED**` whose enforcement was already built — extracted **0 candidate tokens from 21 of 21 already-built stale rows and printed nothing at all**. Its silence was byte-identical to the silence of a genuinely clean queue, so the ledger's standing work queue could be **100% false** (185 rows parsed, 21 `**UNENFORCED**`, 21/21 stale, 0 genuinely open) while its purpose-built detector reported green. This is the STRONGER form of **L155** (a low count is precision evidence, not recall): knowing the recall internally is not enough — the figure must be emitted **in the warning text**, so a reader of the output can tell "found nothing" from "cannot see most of the population". Recall measured against a frozen fixture of the 21 rows, `tests/fixtures/lessons_unenforced_21_2026-07-27.md` (byte-identical copies), measured 2026-07-27: `python3 scripts/invariants.py --full` prints the figure on stderr; the 185/21/0 census command is on L188's row. | **test** — `tests/test_stale_unenforced_advisory.py` (49 tests) pins the recall figure itself (7-of-21, and the per-matcher split), the exact blind-spot ID set as a named constant, and the presence of the coverage-limit sentence in the emitted warning — so the number cannot silently drift and the disclaimer cannot be dropped. | L190 | 2026-07-27 | **A queue that only a machine can close needs a MACHINE-READABLE close marker — enforcement status flipped in prose is unparseable — and a precision requirement has a measurable recall cost that must be published alongside it.** Twenty-one ledger rows were closed in substance by later runs, but each closure lived only as prose ("built by a later run", "supersedes L…"), which no scanner can distinguish from a mention; consequently the rows stayed on the queue forever and the detector had no way to stop re-reporting a row a human had already adjudicated. The fix is ONE canonical marker in the ENFORCEMENT column only — case-sensitive literal `DISPOSES:`, then `L<digits>` tokens separated by commas and/or spaces, terminating at the first non-separator token (a `.` ends the list); a prose mention of an ID NEVER suppresses. The second half of the lesson is the cost: making the `agent_charter` matcher precise (it only fires when the named charter file actually cites the row's own ID) means it MISSES rows whose charter does not self-cite — **measured: 22 of the 164 non-`UNENFORCED` rows name a charter that does not cite their own ID**. That number is published with the matcher, because an unpublished precision/recall tradeoff reads to the next agent as a bug rather than a decision. | `findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md` (2026-07-27 idle run, policy (a)). Marker grammar documented at `scripts/invariants.py::_lesson_disposed_ids` (docstring) and first used by **L188**. Precision-cost figure measured 2026-07-27 over `kb/lessons/00-lessons.md` (164 non-`UNENFORCED` rows at measurement time; the count is corpus-relative and will move as the ledger grows — per **L161/L162** it is quoted with its command and its measurement time, not as a standing fact): `python3 scripts/invariants.py --full` (advisory text) and the matcher tests below. | **invariant (advisory) + test** — the marker is parsed by `scripts/invariants.py::_lesson_disposed_ids` and consumed by `_stale_unenforced_candidate_issues()`; grammar and the "prose mention never suppresses" rule are pinned case-by-case in `tests/test_stale_unenforced_advisory.py`, as is the published precision cost. Enforcement-column-only scoping is deliberate: a `DISPOSES:` written in the lesson-text column must NOT suppress, or a quoted example would silently close rows. NOT statically assertable: whether a disposition is CORRECT — the marker records that a human/verifier adjudicated the row, it cannot check the adjudication. This is the shape that turns a measurement into a false conclusion: the audit re-read 21 rows very carefully and never asked whether 21 was the right denominator. So the "zero closures" reading inverted the truth. Rule: when you add a machine-readable form of a convention that already exists in prose, the FIRST number you publish is its coverage of the existing corpus (how many prose closures the new grammar can see, and how many it cannot), not its own hit count — otherwise the new grammar's emptiness gets read as the domain's emptiness. | L201 | 2026-07-27 | **A regression test may pin a LIVE append-only document's STRUCTURE; it may never pin that document's ENUMERATION.** "Rows X..Z have property P and no other row does" is an assertion about PROSE — it makes the document's author a hostage of the gate. This run's own fix for **L152** shipped exactly that shape: a test asserting, against the LIVE `kb/lessons/00-lessons.md`, that a named set of rows contains pipe characters and that no other row does. It went red twice in one day and forced a kb-distiller to **REWORD ITS OWN LESSON TEXT** to get the binding gate green — the gate editing the knowledge it exists to protect. The repair: every content-enumerating assertion was moved onto a frozen fixture, and the live-tree coverage was replaced by a purely STRUCTURAL contract (nothing dropped, split lossless, every enforcement cell starts at the head of the 5th cell and runs to end-of-row, `n_rows >= 190`), which is true of ANY prose a future row may contain. Structure is an assertion about CODE and survives arbitrary appends; enumeration is an assertion about WORDS and cannot. This is the operational form of **L192**/**L200**, and it fired inside this run's own fix for **L152**. | This run's final code fix, 2026-07-27; `findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md`; frozen fixture `tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md` (49 rows, `grep -c "^\| L" tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md` → `49`, measured 2026-07-27 19:18Z); live ledger `grep -c "^\| L" kb/lessons/00-lessons.md` → `198` rows at the same instant. | L202 | 2026-07-27 | **When freezing a counterfactual, freeze the NEGATIVE population too.** A snapshot containing only the rows that BREAK the naive rule proves "the naive rule breaks these" while silently dropping the anti-tautology half of the claim — "and it agrees with the correct parser everywhere else". A full census of committed tape (1,140,735 lines, all families) finds it is **not confined to perp_tape and not off-by-one**: **7 collided item groups across 3 families** — `perp_tape` `20260717T010032Z` (a `--backfill-funding` one-shot and a scheduled pass, differing in `mode`/`n_prints`/`start_ts`), `econ_prints` `20260716T092842Z` (**all 5** series `KXCPI`/`KXCPICORE`/`KXCPIYOY`/`KXGDP`/`KXPAYROLLS` written twice, 414ms apart), and `anomalies` `20260714T091958Z` (two byte-identical summary rows 84ms apart, so a consumer summing `n_anomalies` double-counts 324). The repo already contains a live consumer of the flawed key: `scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::aggregate_family` counts distinct passes by `capture_id`, so its UNDER-CAPTURE ratio undercounts passes by exactly the collision count. **The load-bearing design finding is what NOT to check.** The obvious rule — one `capture_id` must map to one `captured_at` — is WRONG and would fire a false positive on `hf_burst`, whose single committed capture_id legitimately spans 10 strikes over 1.3s because one ladder round stamps each row as it is fetched. The correct discriminator is whether the **same logical item repeats** under one id: a pass walking a ladder visits each item once, two colliding passes revisit every item they share. Burst families are additionally exempted **structurally**, on the presence of a within-pass sequence field (`capture_seq`/`capture_mono_ns`/`round_index`), never on a family name-list, so a future burst collector inherits the exemption with no edit to the detector. | Census + detector this run over committed `tape/` (read-only, no network); L210's original `perp_tape` observation. | **disposition (machine-readable)** — DISPOSES: L210. Non-gating because these are historical properties of already-committed append-only tape that no run can retroactively repair. | L228 | 2026-07-29 | **A per-venue fee must be a SCHEDULE FUNCTION of the crossed leg's own price, never a flat per-contract constant — and a fee fix applied to one probe MUST be propagated to its sibling schemas in the same run.** `scripts/s17_leadlag_probe.py`'s burst dislocation scan charged the Polymarket leg a flat `--poly-fee` defaulting to **0.0** for **16 days** after `scripts/s9_leadlag_probe.py` (the WC-schema sibling) was corrected to `core.pricing.polymarket_fee_per_contract` on 2026-07-15 (Q31) — and the WC-semi2 finding's own lesson candidate had already written the rule ("must be a rate, not a flat constant"). The stale flat parameter is wrong in BOTH directions: `0.0` under-charges and manufactures phantom dislocations (the prime-directive failure mode), while a naive flat `0.05` over-charges ~4x at mid prices (the real schedule `0.05·p·(1−p)` is **$0.0125** at p=0.50, no cent round-up — the L5 maker/taker error mirrored). It was **load-bearing on the verdict, not cosmetic**: on one identical tape the three views give **0 / 34 / 49** fee-clearing captures (`flat 0.05` / `schedule` / `free`), i.e. the two mis-specified views disagree completely. Corollary: a silently-ignored fee flag is how this survived, so passing `--poly-fee` under a non-`flat` model is now a hard CLI error, not a no-op. | `findings/2026-07-29-s17-burst-fomc-q19.md` §4a/§4c/§4d (lesson candidate #1). Counts produced by `python3 scripts/s17_leadlag_probe.py --burst-window 2026-07-29T17:40:00Z 2026-07-29T19:35:00Z --release-instant 2026-07-29T18:00:00Z --poly-fee-model {schedule,free,flat}`, executed by the research-lead session 2026-07-29 (this worker's and the verifier's sandboxes both refuse `python3`); tape shape re-measured by this distiller 2026-07-30 via `wc -l tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/dt=2026-07-29.jsonl` → **326** and `grep -c capture_summary tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/dt=2026-07-29.jsonl` → **26** (so 300 pair records). | **test** — `tests/test_s17_leadlag_probe.py::test_poly_fee_model_schedule_charges_the_real_schedule_not_a_flat_rate` (asserts $0.0125 at p=0.50 and NOT $0.05), `::test_default_poly_fee_model_is_the_schedule`, `::test_dislocation_scan_hit_count_depends_on_the_fee_model`, `::test_cli_refuses_a_flat_poly_fee_under_a_non_flat_model`. **UNENFORCED** as the general rule: `no_handrolled_fee_rate` in `scripts/invariants.py` only catches banned rate LITERALS, so a flat `*_fee` parameter with a `0.0` default subtracted straight from a P&L trips nothing; candidate — flag any file that subtracts a `*_fee`-named scalar from a net-edge expression without it passing through a `core.pricing` schedule function, plus a sibling-propagation check (two probes reading the same venue must resolve fees through the same helper). |
| L229 | 2026-07-29 | **L27's magnitude gate applies to a CORRELATION and to a stability RATIO, not just to a P&L: a ratio-based robustness check is meaningless against a near-zero denominator.** `leadlag_stability()` labelled `KXFEDDECISION-26SEP-H0` **`stable`** on `rho_full = -0.004471504399603508` (\|ρ\| = 0.0045, pure noise) *because* the leave-one-out recompute moved it FURTHER from zero (−0.040873388781476276), giving `retention = 9.14` — and the printed summary then read "1 of 6 leads survives leave-one-out", promoting the artifact to the headline a future run would quote. Fixed with a named `LEADLAG_RHO_MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` (= the module's own `LEADLAG_SIGNED_LEADER_MARGIN` = 0.05, so the gate is **derived from the code, not fitted to the data**: `per_ticker_leadlag` already refuses to name a leader unless the two lag-ρ *differ* by 0.05, so accepting a smaller ABSOLUTE magnitude as a stable lead is internally incoherent) and a distinct `UNSTABLE_below_magnitude_floor` verdict checked **BEFORE** sign and retention, because magnitude decides whether those two measure anything at all. The inflated ratio is still recorded so the pathology stays legible. Honest headline after the fix: **0 of 6**, not 1 of 6. Generalises: **any ratio-based robustness check needs an absolute-magnitude precondition on its denominator**, and the rejected ratio should be published beside the rejection. | `findings/2026-07-29-s17-burst-fomc-q19.md` §A6/§5 (defect D2, lesson candidate #2); `core.bootstrap.clears_tick_magnitude` / L27 as the P&L-side ancestor. | **test** — `tests/test_s17_leadlag_probe.py::test_leadlag_stability_noise_level_rho_is_not_stable_regression_26sep_h0` (pins the exact real floats), `::test_leadlag_rho_magnitude_floor_is_named_and_tied_to_the_signed_leader_margin`, `::test_leadlag_stability_magnitude_floor_is_checked_before_sign_and_retention`, `::test_print_burst_report_counts_a_below_floor_rho_as_not_surviving`. **UNENFORCED** as the general rule (no static check asserts that every retention/ratio gate in the repo carries a magnitude precondition); candidate — grep for `retention`/ratio-shaped robustness gates and require each to name a floor constant, the same shape `clears_tick_magnitude` already enforces on the P&L side. |
| L230 | 2026-07-29 | **L164 needs an interruption clause: it constrains where a seam is PLANNED, not where one LANDS — and a healthy median gap HIDES seams, so a cadence report must ENUMERATE them.** L164 was written on 2026-07-25 about exactly this event: a uniform chunk plan put a seam on 18:00:00Z, a verifier caught it, and the fix shipped as the hand-verified non-uniform sequence `[16,14,14,14,14,12]`. **The fix was correct and the outcome still went wrong** — a chunk died and restarted, and the FOMC statement was swallowed by a **720.000196 s** hole (nearest captures **−557.175109 s / +162.825087 s**, `release_instant_bracketed = FALSE`) inside a window whose **median gap is a healthy 90.000389 s**. Seam-safe *planning* is necessary and not sufficient; the recipe needs a **restart policy that re-anchors relative to the protected instant**, and every burst finding needs the mechanical bracketing check rather than a median-gap glance. | `findings/2026-07-29-s17-burst-fomc-q19.md` §1/§1a (lesson candidate #4); supersedes nothing in L164 — it extends it. | L231 | 2026-07-29 | **A decision-event capture window must BRACKET the release, because the venue DELISTS the decided market at the decision — and the control that proves it is the venue's own universe count, not the matcher.** All five `KXFEDDECISION-26JUL-*` buckets (the meeting this statement decided) are present at all 7 pre-release in-window captures and **absent from every capture after 18:02:42Z**: 15 pair records/capture pre-release, 10 post, **ZERO post-release observations of the decided market**. So a window that starts at the release, or that loses the release instant to a seam, can *never* observe the decided market's reaction — the observation window for a Kalshi decision market closes at the decision itself. **The competing explanation for five vanishing pairs is always that the PAIR broke** (Polymarket book gone, or the ticker↔slug matcher stopped resolving), which would be a fixable collection defect rather than a permanent foreclosure. Ruled out from the `capture_summary` rows: `n_kalshi_markets` drops **65 → 60** while `n_polymarket_markets` stays **15**, and the five Polymarket ids **1654956-1654960** — confirmed to be exactly the 26JUL pairs' own `market_id`s — move into `unmatched_polymarket` on **all 15** post-release captures. **Polymarket kept the market listed; Kalshi delisted it.** No matcher improvement recovers an observation Kalshi does not publish. Generalises beyond FOMC to **every settle-on-event family**. | `findings/2026-07-29-s17-burst-fomc-q19.md` §2 (lesson candidate #5); membership drop printed by the probe's own `per_capture_pair_counts`; the delisting-vs-matcher control established independently in the verifier round. Tape check re-run by this distiller 2026-07-30: `grep -c 'KXFEDDECISION-26JUL' tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/dt=2026-07-29.jsonl` → **50** = 5 buckets × 10 captures, none of them post-release. | **protocol** — the venue's delisting behaviour is not assertable from our side (an honest terminal state, L28 family: verify the window is observable before building verdict machinery). The two assertable halves ARE built: per-capture membership reporting (`per_capture_pair_counts`, pinned by `tests/test_s17_leadlag_probe.py::test_per_capture_pair_counts_names_a_market_that_disappears`) so a disappearance is IN the report instead of hand-derived, and the L230 bracketing check. Standing probe-design rule, recorded here and cited by Q19's blocking condition: **read `n_<venue>_markets` plus the `unmatched_<venue>` list before blaming the matcher** — that pair distinguishes a venue delisting from a match failure in one read. |

| L233 | 2026-07-29 | **A hand-derived exhaustiveness bound is a SCREEN, not a proof, unless every minimum in it was READ OFF THE DATA — and the agreement between a hand derivation and execution is evidence about the ARITHMETIC, never about the CANDIDATE SET.** Round 1 presented an exhaustiveness proof for the dislocation census that smuggled in two fee minima that do not hold on this tape: a minimum Polymarket fee of ≈$0.0004 (true minimum here ≈$0.00005, at `pb = 0.001`) and a minimum Kalshi fee of $0.01 on **both** crossed legs — but `core.pricing.fee_per_contract` returns **$0.00**, not $0.01, on a leg priced 0.00, and four buckets quote a 0.00 `yes_bid`. The corrected direction-B bound admits gaps down to ≈0.01005, so the `\|gap\| ≥ 0.02` hand pass (102 rows) was **not** the superset it was claimed to be. The result survived only because an independent verifier ran a **fully exhaustive 96-distinct-quote-tuple census** requiring no bound at all and landed on the same floats. **A fee floor that "can never be zero" usually can.** Second half, equally load-bearing: execution reproduced the hand-derived headline EXACTLY (34 captures / 5 episodes / max +$0.008220000000000035 vs a hand derivation of 34 / 5 / +$0.00822), which is real evidence about the arithmetic and **none whatever** about completeness — a hand derivation and the script it mirrors can share the same missed region, and here they did. | `findings/2026-07-29-s17-burst-fomc-q19.md` §4c + §STATUS (verifier defect 3, lesson candidates #8/#9); the 96-tuple census is the verifier's own enumeration, produced without execution (its sandbox refuses `python3`). | **protocol** — methodology judgment, an honest terminal state: no static check can tell an assumed minimum from a measured one. Encoded as probe discipline (`.claude/agents/edge-prober.md` / `verifier` two-agent rule) and as the label convention this finding now uses — a bound is published as a **screen validated ex post** unless every constant in it cites the file it was measured from. Sibling of L9 (verify the premise from the data before joining on it). |

| L235 | 2026-07-29 | **An argmax over a SIGNED statistic can crown a negative one, and a magnitude floor only catches that by luck — a sign precondition and a magnitude gate are independent gates, neither implying the other.** `per_ticker_leadlag`'s `signed_leader` maximises the signed lag-ρ, so when both directions are negative it names the **less negative** one the leader: `KXFEDDECISION-26SEP-H0` got `leader = polymarket` on ρ = **−0.0045** over Kalshi's **−0.2536**, because −0.0045 > −0.2536 as a signed comparison. "Less negative" is not a lead in any economic reading. So clause (i) above is wrong on its own facts: the three residues are the SAME float, and round 2's shared-float claim — which the verifier round "corrected" to ≈3.955e-17 / ≈2.776e-17 — was RIGHT. The PRINCIPLE stands unchanged (residues from different subtractions are in general different, and you only know by evaluating); this particular trio coincides. Clause (ii) and every COUNT reproduce exactly on the committed tape this run: per-observation 5 residue captures, per-episode-max 3 episodes / 4 captures, complement 44 of 49 — note the text's "3 episodes / 5 captures" mixes the two views, the honest pairing is 3 episodes / 4 captures (max view) vs 5 captures (per-observation). | L237 | 2026-07-29 | **An orchestrating session's `git reset --hard` destroys a worker's uncommitted edits — and a worker whose sandbox cannot execute `python3` should ship code plus a BOUNDED hand derivation for the orchestrator to EXECUTE, never to accept.** This milestone took **5 worker rounds** for two compounding process reasons, both recorded rather than laundered: (i) a mid-run `git reset --hard` to `origin/main` by the orchestrating session discarded round 1's identical uncommitted source edits, forcing a full redo — so the standing rule is **commit-then-continue** before any long read-only stretch, and re-verify `git diff --stat` after one; and (ii) **both** the prober's and the verifier's sandboxes refused every `python3` and `git` invocation (`"This command requires approval"`, no interactive approver), so the research-lead session executed all three probe runs, both gates and the commit while the workers produced the code and the analysis. That division worked and is the pattern to repeat: round 1's hand-derived headline reproduced EXACTLY under execution, and the verifier — unable to run anything — reproduced every load-bearing number from raw tape with its own parser, including a census **stronger** than the prober's own screen (L233). The failure mode to avoid is the orchestrator *accepting* an unexecuted number as measured; the number classes must stay labelled (`EXECUTED` vs `TAPE-VERIFIED`) in the document itself, as this finding's header does. | `findings/2026-07-29-s17-burst-fomc-q19.md` §STATUS/§Provenance + the final recommendation's commit paragraph (lesson candidate #9); this run's own bookkeeping (this distiller's sandbox likewise refuses `python3` and `git`, so every gate number here is cited to the research-lead session, not re-measured). | **protocol** — sandbox capability and orchestrator git hygiene are not assertable from inside the repo (an honest terminal state). Sibling of L4 (a permission boundary mistaken for a race cost Q1 two builds). |
| L238 | 2026-07-30 | **A window-grid denominator is defined by TWO numbers — width AND anchor — and getting the anchor wrong produces a confident, precisely-wrong answer that the width alone cannot expose.** L208's own worked example (`findings/2026-07-27-perp-tape-audit.md` PERP-F1) named four permanently-lost 8h funding windows in `tape/perp_tape/` — `2026-07-23T08Z`, `07-24T08Z`, `07-25T08Z`, `07-25T16Z`. Those are 00Z-anchored CALENDAR bins over `captured_at`. Kalshi perps' actual funding boundaries, read off the collector's own `next_funding_time` field, land on the **04/12/20Z** grid: **1,534 of 1,534** committed `funding_estimate` rows are on that anchor and **zero** on 00/08/16. On the same tape and the same 33-window span the honest grid has **THREE** zero-pass windows — `07-24T04Z`, `07-25T04Z`, `07-25T20Z` — a set **disjoint** from the four reported (not one instant in common), and 10/33 = 30.3% path-inadequate rather than 12/33 = 36%. The width (8h) was right in both readings, so nothing about the count, the cadence or the span looked wrong; only re-deriving the anchor from the collector's own boundary field exposed it. Rules: (1) a window grid must be enumerated from the field the VENUE publishes the boundary in, never from `captured_at` binned to a wall-clock multiple; (2) a boundary value that is off the assumed grid must be COUNTED AND REPORTED, never snapped to the nearest slot — snapping is exactly what converts a mis-anchored grid into a plausible wrong answer; (3) a defect report naming specific lost windows must cite the boundary field it read them from, because the instants are the claim. | This run's build of L208's own enforcement candidate: `scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::expected_window_grid_coverage` (executed against real committed tape 2026-07-30, and independently re-derived beforehand by a from-scratch scratch script that reproduced BOTH readings — the audit's four 00Z bins exactly, and the three 04Z-grid windows); `findings/2026-07-30-l208-window-grid-coverage.md`. | **test + non-gating advisory** — the anchor is a first-class configured field (`WINDOW_GRIDDED_FAMILIES[...]["anchor_hour_utc"]`) VALIDATED against the data rather than assumed: `_on_grid` is exact to the microsecond and every off-grid boundary is counted in `n_offgrid_window_keys` + surfaced by `scripts/invariants.py::window_grid_coverage_warning` with the explicit instruction to re-read the anchor before trusting any window statistic on that family. Pinned by `tests/test_tape_gap_monitor.py::test_window_grid_offgrid_key_is_reported_never_snapped_L208`, `::test_window_grid_on_grid_helper_is_exact_L208`, and `::test_acceptance_9_l208_perp_tape_funding_grid_frozen_slice` (which asserts the audit's four 00Z-anchored bins are DISJOINT from this grid's zero windows, so a future re-anchoring cannot silently restore the wrong answer), plus `tests/test_invariants.py::test_window_grid_issues_offgrid_key_alone_is_an_issue_L208`. **PROVISIONAL on the two-agent rule:** the correction was produced and adversarially re-derived within ONE session (no `verifier` subagent was dispatchable in this run's harness); `findings/2026-07-27-perp-tape-audit.md` PERP-F1 is therefore annotated, NOT rewritten, and no registry status moved. |
| L239 | 2026-07-30 | **An auditability predicate must key on the EXACT field the deriving function reads — keying on a superset manufactures a "checked" that was never checked.** The L214 census asked "does this record carry the evidence its verdict was derived from?" with `group_item_title or question`, while `collection.polymarket_pairs.fed_bucket_terms`'s Polymarket side reads `group_item_title` and NOTHING ELSE. A record with a null `group_item_title` and a rich `question` therefore reported `has_terms: True` sitting right beside `polymarket_basis: None` — the census would have certified as "terms present and checked" exactly the records whose basis the deriving function could not parse, i.e. it would have over-counted auditability in the flattering direction. General form: "has evidence" is not a semantic question about the record, it is a mechanical question about the READER — write the predicate against the reader's own field list, and if the two ever diverge the census is measuring a different thing than the function. | `findings/2026-07-30-l214-crossvenue-terms-provenance.md`; independent `verifier` round 1, defect D3 (this run) | **test** — `tests/test_polymarket_pair_terms_audit.py::test_question_text_alone_is_not_evidence_of_a_checked_basis` (a record with `question` set and `group_item_title` null must NOT count as auditable) and `::test_classify_record_requires_both_the_block_and_its_evidence`. Remaining as **protocol**: nothing statically ties a future predicate to a future deriving function's field list. |

| L241 | 2026-07-30 | **A public verdict function must VERIFY the join key it presupposes, not inherit it from its only caller — and when it cannot verify it, it must say so in the record rather than claim a silent pass.** `fed_bucket_terms` compares two legs' bps regions. It was safe only because its one in-repo caller, `match_fed_pairs`, joins on `meeting_key` first — but it is an importable public function, and called directly with a Sept-2026 Kalshi title (`"Will the Federal Reserve Hike rates by 25bps at their September 2026 meeting?"`) against a Jan-2099 Polymarket leg it returned `terms_equivalent: True`: identical bps regions at two different meetings, which settle on different days and are not the same contract. Fixed three ways, all persisted: the function compares `meeting_key` explicitly (mismatch ⇒ `False` with a note naming both keys), it emits `meeting_key_checked: True/False` so a caller that supplied no meeting keys is never told the join was verified, and the block names its own scope with `compares: "bps_region+meeting_key"`. The scope string is load-bearing: text equivalence is not settlement equivalence when the adjudicators differ (`kalshi_rulebook` vs `uma_oracle`, persisted per leg), so even a `True` leaves adjudicator risk unmeasured and no text comparison can settle it. | `findings/2026-07-30-l214-crossvenue-terms-provenance.md`; independent `verifier` round 1 (defect: unverified join-key presupposition) + round 2 re-derivation (this run) | **test** — `tests/test_polymarket_pairs.py::test_fed_bucket_terms_different_meetings_are_not_equivalent` (the Sept-2026-vs-Jan-2099 case, pinned as `False` with the mismatch note) and `::test_fed_bucket_terms_absent_meeting_key_is_flagged_not_claimed` (`meeting_key_checked: False`, never a silent pass). Verifier round 2 additionally fuzzed **1,764 combinations** and found **0** cases of `terms_equivalent: True` with both meeting keys present and differing, and confirmed the fix is verdict-NEUTRAL on the 20 already-written v2 lines. Remaining as **protocol**: nothing statically forces the next verdict function to declare its own `compares` scope. |

Same family as L165 (a citation must name the artifact it was read from) and L161 (a bare number drifts as the repo grows), but unlike L165 this one IS mechanizable. | `findings/2026-07-30-l214-crossvenue-terms-provenance.md`; independent `verifier` round 2, defect 1 (this run) | **test (BUILT 2026-07-30, research loop, idle-run policy (a); lesson TEXT unchanged — only this enforcement cell moves, per the L152 own-row-update rule).** Built exactly the candidate this row named: `scripts/polymarket_pair_terms_audit.py::resolve_git_ref()` (local `git rev-parse HEAD`, `None` on any git/subprocess failure — never raises, never poisons the rest of the report) and `::count_pair_records_at_ref()` (local `git ls-tree`/`git show` at that ref, counting only `PAIR_SCHEMAS` lines that are actually committed there — ignores any uncommitted working-tree lines; `None` when `tape_dir` isn't inside `repo_root` or the ref/git call fails). `audit()` now reports `git_ref` and `n_records_at_head` alongside the existing working-tree-only `n_pair_records`, plus a `provenance_note` field spelling out which counts describe the working tree vs. `git_ref`. Both helpers take an injectable `run_git` callable (same pattern as `scripts/tape_branch_sweep.py::default_git_runner`) so no test needs to touch the real repo's history. 9 new tests in `tests/test_polymarket_pair_terms_audit.py`: a real (throwaway, isolated-identity, `commit.gpgsign=false`) git repo per test proves an appended-but-uncommitted line is excluded from `n_records_at_head` while still counted in `n_pair_records`; a `run_git` that raises proves both new fields degrade to `None` without disturbing the rest of the report; `tape_dir` outside `repo_root` and a bad ref both return `None`; and a real-tree acceptance test compares `audit()`'s `git_ref` against a freshly-queried `git rev-parse HEAD` (never hardcoded). | L243 | 2026-07-30 | **Two reporting rules a "schema bump + tape append" pass must follow, because the two effects of such a diff are always confounded in the default invocation.** (i) **"No probe output changed" is only checkable TAPE-CONSTANT.** The honest claim is: held tape-constant (old code vs new code on a frozen tape, via a `git worktree` differential), q48 / q31 / s17 outputs are byte-identical. It is FALSE to say "no probe's numeric output changed", because the same diff also appends 20 records, which moves every default invocation — q48 `n_fed_records` 9,555 → 9,575, `n_passes` 640 → 641, `mean_abs` 0.0206128 → 0.0206215, headline `all` +2.061¢ → **+2.062¢**; q31 obs 15,905 → 15,925, pooled mean net −0.03244 → **−0.03246**, 95% CI [−0.04006,−0.02540] → **[−0.04007,−0.02541]**; s17 contemporaneous ρ 0.43582 → 0.43724, kalshi-leads ρ 0.019706 → 0.015051 (all prices `price_source_tag: real_ask`, 9,575/9,575 records; DESCRIPTIVE/diagnostic statistics, **not** a strategy CI and **not** a P&L). State the two deltas separately, with numbers, or the code claim silently absorbs the data claim. (ii) **When a verdict is a deterministic function of a parsed label, the RECORD count is not a sample size.** The 20 v2 records look like n=20 but come from ONE capture pass, and `terms_equivalent` is a pure function of the parsed threshold-basis pair `(kalshi_basis, polymarket_basis)` — NOT of the literal (title, label) pair, which is distinct on all 20 only because the Kalshi title embeds the meeting month. Collapsing on the parsed bases gives **5 bucket classes** (3 equivalent, 2 provably not), n=4 each: 20 = 5 classes × 4 meetings × 1 pass. And the two non-equivalent classes are the SAME `>25bps`-vs-`>=50bps` rule mirrored hike/cut, so the number of **independent asymmetry mechanisms is 1, not 2** — say so, or a reader takes "2" as corroboration. Reporting either number without its collapse is pseudo-replication (L6's unit rule, applied to a parser instead of a bootstrap). | `findings/2026-07-30-l214-crossvenue-terms-provenance.md`; independent `verifier` round 1 defect 2 (probe-impact wording) + round 2 (unit-count/pseudo-replication wording), this run | **protocol** — neither half is statically assertable: (i) requires knowing which tape a claim was made against (a claim about a sentence, not about code), and (ii) requires knowing the equivalence class the verdict function actually keys on, which varies per function. Encoded where it binds: this row, the finding's own "tape-constant vs data-driven" section, and LOOP-QUEUE's Q48 Status line. Adjacent to L162 (a count claim carries its command + measurement time) and L6 (bootstrap the independent unit, never the row). |
| L244 | 2026-07-31 | **A self-activation gate that counts DAYS must bound its window at BOTH ends and whitelist the instrument grammar it means — an unbounded season predicate plus an unfiltered family tape lets markets that can never settle in the study window buy the gate days it did not earn.** Q37's `_summer_contract_days_available()` reported 19 summer daily contract-days where only 17 existed. Two compounding defects, each individually harmless-looking: `is_summer(d)` was `d >= SUMMER_START` with no upper bound (so a 2028-12-31 contract day is "summer 2026"), and `load_daily_snapshots()` had no series whitelist (so `tape/weather_books/`, which is captured by weather FAMILY not by ladder SHAPE, fed `KXARCTICICEMIN-26OCT01-*` and `KXTXURI-28DEC31-*` into a daily-temperature-ladder population). Each contaminant bought exactly one phantom gate day — enough to fire the >=21 gate two calendar days early on 19 real days of tape, a 10% shortfall against the gate's own design, silently. The population contamination was small (10 of 2,289 trades, 0.44%); the GATE contamination was the damage. Generalization for every gated probe in this repo: a day-count gate is an argument about which days COUNT, so it must be tested against rows that are the right shape on the wrong day AND the wrong shape on the right day, not just the happy case. | L253 | 2026-08-01 | **A fill proxy that cannot observe trade direction CANNOT exhibit adverse selection — so a non-negative post-fill markout measured off a depth-only tape is the expected output of a blind instrument, never evidence that adverse selection is absent.** Q50 instrumented the S68 fill-sim with a post-fill markout (the book's own mid `k` snapshots after the simulated fill, minus our fill price) and measured it *positive and non-decaying* — H=24/N=1 gives k=0 **+0.0543**, k=1 +0.0562, k=5 +0.0610, k=10 +0.0618. Read naively that says "no adverse selection", which would be a remarkable claim. It is an artifact of the instrument: `tape/orderbook_depth/` carries no trade or volume field (L68/L106), so the `touch` rule counts a CANCEL at our price identically to a TRADE against us, and a rule blind to the direction of the crossing flow has no channel through which adverse selection could appear. The +5.4c at k=0 is approximately the half-spread of these wide books — a restatement of "we bought at the bid", not a measured informational cost. The companion measure that IS informative is the **break-even haircut**: how large a per-filled-leg charge flips the verdict. On Q50's ten CI>0 cells it is **0.5c-4.0c per filled leg**, i.e. one-to-four times Kalshi's flat 1c maker fee — so the entire verdict is decided by a term the tape cannot measure. Any maker probe on a depth-only tape must publish that break-even and must NOT publish a markout as reassurance. | Q50/S68 build, 2026-08-01 (`scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::leg_markout` docstring + `::adverse_selection_breakeven`; `findings/2026-08-01-q50-s68-tighter-gate-rederivation.md`) | **test (BUILT 2026-08-01, same run).** `adverse_selection_breakeven()` is computed and printed for every candidate cell, and the blindness caveat is written into `leg_markout`'s docstring and into the report's own ADVERSE SELECTION block so it cannot be read without it. Pinned by `tests/test_q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::test_markout_is_negative_when_the_book_moves_against_us` (sign convention on both sides), `::test_breakeven_haircut_is_found_and_charges_two_legs_on_a_double_fill`, `::test_breakeven_ladder_is_monotone_non_increasing_in_mean`. NOT wired into `invariants.py` — which probe owes a break-even is a per-probe judgment, not a tree property. Resolves only when a trade-bearing feed exists (Q47 `orderbook_delta`, Ryan-gated). |
| L254 | 2026-08-01 | **An apparent maker edge that is MONOTONE INCREASING in the entry horizon and INVERTS to negative in the only genuinely near-close window is a fillability artifact, not an edge — so a maker probe must report its verdict as a function of entry horizon, never at a single H.** Q50 swept 7 entry horizons x 5 gate widths (35 cells). All 10 CI>0 cells sit at H>=24h and nine of them at H>=48h; the two genuinely near-close windows are NEGATIVE at the adequately-powered cut (H=6h mean **−0.0562**, 14 series; H=12h **−0.0397**, 15 series), and the point estimate rises monotonically with H (6h −0.056 -> 24h +0.070 -> 72h +0.151). **MECHANISM CORRECTED 2026-08-01 by the Q50 verifier — the horizon pattern is REAL, the original attribution was NOT. WITHDRAWN original wording, kept visible because this ledger does not silently rewrite rows: *"This is the S29 DEAD-by-fillability signature reproduced on the same tape family: nominal bids on days-out, effectively one-sided books that a generous fill-sim still 'fills' (L31/L48)."* That sentence is not supported by the data.** The pattern survives a matched-ticker control: on the 70 tickers / 14 series present at BOTH H=12 and H=72 the estimate still flips, CI [−0.1137,−0.0054] -> +0.0151, so it is not population turnover. But the ALIVE cell is not a days-out nominal book — median entry ttc at H=24/N=1 is **23.7h**, and its queue-ahead/capturable-depth numbers look like an actually-traded book. The real driver is a **SPREAD REGIME**: median entry spread is **$0.020 (2 ticks — exactly the Q49 fee boundary)** at the near-close H=6/N=0 and H=12/N=0 cells, versus **$0.060-$0.090 (6-9 ticks)** at 9 of the 10 ALIVE cells (the tenth, H=72/N=5, is wider still at $0.290) (per-cell `median_entry_spread` in `reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_summary.json`). The wide-spread population this ladder exists to find **simply does not exist near close** — H=6/N=1 admits only 25 candidates across 4 series, below the L41 10-series floor. So the near-close cells are negative because they are a DIFFERENT, tighter-spread population, not because the same books stop filling; the honest residue at the near-close end is data-adequacy, and "fillability" is a statement about the days-out end only. Practical addendum to the rule: publish the per-cell **median entry spread** next to the horizon ladder, because "no near-close sibling" may mean "that population does not exist there," which is a different (and weaker) kill than "it exists and does not fill." Had Q50 reported only its most-populated cell (H=72h, 423 candidates / 17 series, CI [+0.0200,+0.1587]) it would have published a spurious ALIVE on the LEAST fillable population in the sweep — sample size and fillability point in opposite directions here, so "pick the biggest n" is exactly the wrong tiebreak. | Q50/S68 build, 2026-08-01 (`findings/2026-08-01-q50-s68-tighter-gate-rederivation.md` ladder table); mechanism corrected the same day by the Q50 verifier pass (matched-ticker H=12 ∩ H=72 control; per-cell `median_entry_spread` now emitted by `scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::run`) | **test (BUILT 2026-08-01, same run).** `scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py` sweeps `DEFAULT_HORIZONS_H = (6,12,18,24,36,48,72)` by default and prints EVERY cell with its own verdict — a single-H run is possible only by explicitly passing `--horizons`. `tests/test_q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::test_entry_window_shifts_the_simulated_ladders` pins that a later entry simulates over strictly fewer snapshots (the mechanism behind the horizon dependence). Generalizes to every future maker/fill-sim probe: publish the horizon ladder, and treat a positive cell that has no near-close sibling as an artifact until shown otherwise. |

That is decisive — it means the ladder's positive cells are an arithmetic restatement of `half-spread x fill-rate − fees` on a wide-spread population, which is true by construction of the entry gate, and NOT a claim that the fills pick winners. Plumbed into every trade row (`pnl_strategy_mid_as_truth`, `mid_at_fill`, `mid_price_source_tag`) and every ladder cell. **The operative half is in `cell_verdict`, as a ONE-WAY override:** a cell that clears both L256 exit treatments is then checked against the control, and if the control reproduces its positive CI the cell is returned `DEAD-by-zero-information-control`; the control is a REQUIRED key on that path (it RAISES rather than defaulting to "no control, therefore alive") and is never consulted in the other direction — it can remove an ALIVE, never award one. **MEASURED ON COMMITTED TAPE (full 35-cell ladder, `python3 scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py`, n_boot=10,000, seed 42) — the CONTROL CIs below are `PROVISIONAL` under the two-agent verdict rule: no `verifier` subagent was dispatchable in this harness, so they carry producer + independent-code-path confirmation only (residual (iv)). Headline cell H=24h/N=1: hold mean **+$0.0695** CI **[+0.0235, +0.1473]** vs zero-information control mean **+$0.0494** CI **[+0.0026, +0.1019]** (`real_bid` entries, `midpoint` control payout; n=100 trades / 13 series). Across the ten cells the control's mean lands between 71% and 125% of the hold mean, and **0 of 1,855 orphan legs anywhere in the ladder had an unquotable mid**, so the control speaks for exactly the population it is a control for. No registry row moves and no verdict changes (`DEAD across every (N,H) cell`; S68 stays `dead ✗`) — the control makes the EXISTING kill stronger and reproducible, it does not create a new one. **Pinned by 20 new test functions / 24 test items in `tests/test_q50_s68_gate_ladder.py`** (83 items in the file, was 59): the payout direction and its $1 exhaustion, the unmeasurable-not-zero rule, non-probability/unknown-side rejection, `::test_mid_as_truth_pnl_is_the_immediate_markout_minus_the_entry_maker_fee` (the identity that stops the control and the L253 markout drifting apart), `::test_mid_as_truth_pnl_charges_the_maker_rate_not_the_taker_rate` (L5), `::test_orphan_control_is_settlement_independent_unlike_the_hold_branch` (the same orphan is +$0.69 or −$0.31 under hold and the SAME number either way under the control), `::test_the_control_is_not_the_flatten_treatment` (two DIFFERENT counterfactuals — if they coincided by construction one of them would have stopped saying anything), `::test_unquotable_mid_is_dropped_from_the_control_bootstrap_not_zeroed` (L86), `::test_control_reproduces_only_when_both_lower_bounds_are_positive`, `::test_cell_verdict_downgrades_an_alive_cell_whose_control_reproduces_it`, `::test_control_can_only_remove_an_alive_never_award_one`, `::test_cell_verdict_refuses_to_report_alive_without_the_control`, and two HARD real-tape acceptance tests — `::test_acceptance_l255_zero_information_control_reproduces_the_headline_cell` (re-derived live off committed tape, NON-VACUITY asserted FIRST so it fails loudly if the hold branch ever stops being positive) and `::test_acceptance_l255_control_reproduces_every_hold_alive_cell_in_the_committed_ladder` (the full 10/10 claim + zero unmeasurable coverage against the committed summary). **NOT DONE / RESIDUAL.** (i) NOT promoted to a repo-wide `invariants.py` gate: what counts as "zero information" is per-probe (mid-as-truth here; a shuffled-outcome or de-vigged-prior control elsewhere), exactly as this row's original cell says — the assertable half is the narrow per-module absence-of-fallback above. (ii) The override currently never FIRES on this tape, because L256's flatten branch kills all ten cells before the control is consulted; its value here is evidentiary (the per-cell comparison is published for every cell) plus prospective — see **L258**. (iii) The `turnover` fill model reports no control at all (no localised fill instant → no snapshot whose mid to read; honest None + counted, L86) — acceptable because it is a labeled diagnostic only (L250). (iv) Single-agent run: no `verifier` subagent was dispatchable in this harness, so the numbers above were re-derived by a second INDEPENDENT code path reading only `reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_rows.jsonl` (own fee function, own grouping, own bootstrap, different seed/n_boot): all **4,727** persisted per-row control P&Ls re-derived with **0** mismatches, 33/34 cells reproduced the mean EXACTLY with an identical CI-sign classification, and the 10/10 reproduction claim held. The single disagreement (H=24/N=3, a cell that is NOT one of the ten) is a bootstrap-seed boundary artifact: the control lower bound there sits at ~0 (+5.6e-18 independently vs −0.00020 published), i.e. the sign classification of a CI whose bound IS zero to numerical noise. |
| L256 | 2026-08-01 | **A fill-sim that can leave an UNHEDGED SINGLE-SIDE leg must report BOTH exit treatments — hold-to-settlement AND flatten-at-cross — and a verdict that survives only the generous hold-to-settlement treatment is not a verdict.** Hold-to-settlement marks the orphan leg to the `broker_truth` $0/$1 payout, which is the most generous possible treatment: it grants the sim a free directional lottery ticket that no maker running this strategy would knowingly keep. The honest alternative is to FLATTEN AT THE CROSS — close the orphan by buying the other side at its ask — and on a mirrored binary book where the two prices sum to exactly $1 that exit costs precisely both fees, i.e. `pnl = −(fee_entry + fee_exit)`, a small certain loss instead of a fat lottery. The Q50 verifier applied it to all 10 of Q50's ALIVE cells and **every one died, CI straddling zero** — the entire apparent edge lived in the unhedged legs' settlement lottery. Not statically assertable (the flatten price depends on the book's own quote at the flatten instant, which is a per-probe modelling choice); a natural companion to L32's frozen/movement dual cut, which is already house style for the same reason."* **WHAT WAS BUILT.** `scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py` gains the treatment as re-runnable code, closing this row's own L165 provenance hole (the flattened CIs existed only inside a verifier's hand-run session; they are now in a committed artifact): `taker_fee()` (the crossing leg via `core.pricing.fee_per_contract` at `TAKER_FEE_RATE`, never hand-rolled — L5 with its sign flipped, since charging maker on both legs would make the HONEST branch look better than it is), `opposite_side_ask(rec, side)` (the real `best_no_ask`/`best_yes_ask` at the fill instant; None — never a number — on an absent, $0.00 or off-scale quote, because a zero ask is the ABSENCE of an offer, L1/L105), `flatten_at_cross_pnl(entry_price, side, opposite_ask)` = `(1 - entry - ask) - maker_fee(entry) - taker_fee(ask)`, and `flatten_analysis()` — the same object, same GAME-SERIES unit (L6/L41), same `n_boot`, same admissibility gate as the hold branch, publishing `n_unmeasurable_single_side` because an unquotable flatten is DROPPED from the bootstrap, never booked at $0.00 (L86). Plumbed into every trade row (`pnl_strategy_flatten`, `flatten_price`, `flatten_price_source_tag=real_ask`) and every ladder cell. **The operative half is in `cell_verdict`:** the kill ladder is factored into `branch_verdict()` and run over BOTH treatments, the verdict is `max` by `VERDICT_SEVERITY` (ALIVE ranks least severe, so the WORSE branch always wins), and the function RAISES `KeyError` when the `flatten_at_cross` key is absent — pinning the ABSENCE of a generous-branch fallback is the only thing that stops a future refactor from quietly restoring the treatment this lesson was filed to stop being the headline. **MEASURED ON COMMITTED TAPE (full 35-cell ladder, n_boot=10,000, seed 42, 54s):** 10 cells are ALIVE under hold-to-settlement — exactly the ten this row was written about — and **0 of 10 survive flatten-at-cross**, so the KILL is confirmed and the overall verdict is unchanged (`DEAD across every (N,H) cell`; no registry row moves). **MECHANISM REFINED, not withdrawn:** this row's prose says all ten died "CI straddling zero", which is true of only **three** (H=24/N=1 lower bound −0.0148, H=48/N=0 −0.0036, H=72/N=0 −0.0023). The other **seven** keep a flatten lower bound that is still POSITIVE but SUB-TICK (+0.0006, +0.0010, +0.0023, +0.0027, +0.0034, +0.0058, +0.0060 — a fraction of one cent) and die on L27's tick-magnitude gate instead. Flatten alone would not have killed those seven; flatten AND L27 together do — which is precisely the kind of thing a hand-run derivation reported as prose could not have surfaced. 0 of 1,855 orphan legs across the whole ladder were unquotable, so the flatten branch speaks for the same population as the hold branch here. **Pinned by 13 new test functions / 17 test items in `tests/test_q50_s68_gate_ladder.py`:** `::test_flatten_on_a_mirrored_unmoved_book_costs_exactly_the_two_fees` (the arithmetic identity computed, not hardcoded, on both sides), `::test_flatten_charges_the_taker_rate_on_the_exit_leg_not_the_maker_rate` (L5), `::test_flatten_sign_tracks_which_way_the_book_moved` (sign convention pinned on BOTH sides + monotonicity), `::test_flatten_is_unmeasurable_not_zero_when_the_opposite_ask_is_missing`, `::test_orphan_leg_flatten_is_settlement_independent_unlike_the_hold_branch` (the same orphan is a +$0.69 jackpot or a −$0.31 write-off under hold, and the SAME small certain loss either way when flattened), `::test_hedged_and_unfilled_rows_are_identical_under_the_two_treatments`, `::test_unquotable_orphan_is_dropped_from_the_flatten_bootstrap_not_zeroed`, `::test_turnover_model_reports_no_flatten_because_it_localises_no_fill_instant`, `::test_cell_verdict_takes_the_worse_of_the_two_exit_treatments`, `::test_cell_verdict_refuses_to_score_a_cell_with_no_flatten_branch`, `::test_verdict_severity_ranks_alive_as_the_least_severe_answer`, and two HARD real-tape acceptance tests — `::test_acceptance_l256_flatten_at_cross_kills_the_alive_hold_to_settlement_cell` and `::test_acceptance_l256_every_hold_alive_cell_dies_under_flatten_in_the_committed_ladder` (the full 10/0 claim and the 3-straddle/7-sub-tick split against the committed summary artifact). **NOT DONE / RESIDUAL.** (i) NOT promoted to a repo-wide `invariants.py` gate: which probes owe a flatten branch is a per-probe judgment (a probe with no single-side category owes nothing), and the flatten PRICE is a modelling choice — the assertable half is the narrow per-module absence-of-fallback above. (ii) The `turnover` fill model reports no flatten at all: it accumulates departures over the whole hold and never localises a fill instant, so there is no snapshot at which to read an exit quote; honest None + counted (L86), acceptable because it is a labeled diagnostic only (L250). (iii) The flatten assumes we lift the quote observed at the fill snapshot in unlimited size — no slippage, no partial fill; that is generous in the honest branch's favour and remains uncharged. (v) Single-agent run: no `verifier` subagent was available in this context, so the numbers above were re-derived by a second INDEPENDENT code path (own grouping, own bootstrap, own gates, reading only `reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_rows.jsonl`) which reproduced 10/0 and the 3/7 split exactly; the mechanism refinement is flagged for a second pair of eyes rather than treated as verdict-class (nothing flipped). |
| L257 | 2026-08-01 | **A verifier caveat that names ONE population understates a sampling artifact that spans EVERY population — the artifact belongs beside every labeled cut's `n`, not only the headline's.** Q49/S68's verifier caveat #1 correctly flagged that the PRIMARY `fillable_entry` cut was a tape-start artifact (all 20 entries on one `entry_captured_at`). Recomputing the same statistic over all four of that probe's labeled cuts from committed tape shows the contamination was never confined to the primary: the SAME instant (`2026-07-07T01:23:57.700581+00:00`, the depth tape's first full pass) is the top instant of **all four** cuts, carrying **20/20 (100%)** of `fillable_entry`, **20/20 (100%)** of `nearclose_le_24h`, **156/284 (54.9%)** of `spread_le_10c`, and **210/445 (47.2%)** of `unrestricted` — so even the widest "445 candidates / 18 series" population is nearly half one snapshot. The reason it read as a single-cut problem is that a caveat is written about the number in the headline, while the entry rule that caused it was shared by every cut. Rule: when a sampling artifact is found in one cut, RE-DERIVE it across every cut the write-up reports before deciding its scope — an entry rule is a property of the probe, not of the cut. Honest counterweight recorded with it: `unrestricted`'s 47.2% sits BELOW the descriptor's blunt 0.5 flag, i.e. the flag is not a rubber stamp and a share alone does not settle whether a cluster is an artifact. | This run (2026-08-01 research-loop IDLE RUN, policy (a)); `findings/2026-08-01-l251-entry-instant-concentration.md`. Re-derivable in one command from committed tape via `scripts/q49_s68_bothside_maker_fillsim.py`'s own loaders + `core.bootstrap.entry_instant_concentration`; the exact figures are frozen at `tests/fixtures/q49_entry_instants_2026-08-01.json` and pinned by `tests/test_bootstrap.py::test_acceptance_q49_contamination_is_not_confined_to_the_primary_cut` / `::test_acceptance_the_flag_is_not_a_rubber_stamp_on_real_data`. Q49's verdict (DEAD-by-fee, verifier-CONFIRMED) is UNCHANGED and no registry status moved: the contamination is a sampling-quality fact about the populations, and the finding's own alternative-rule population (176 candidates / 17 series / 131 games) was already DEAD too. **ID NOTE (L161-class provenance):** `L253`-`L256` were claimed by the Q50 verifier-pass branch (`idle-run-a-q50-s68-gate-ladder`, PR #263), which merged to `main` first while this row's branch was rebasing; this row takes `L257` to avoid a silent ID collision with the now-merged content. | **test** — the cross-cut figures are pinned by the two acceptance tests named above against a frozen fixture, and the descriptor that produces them is `core.bootstrap.entry_instant_concentration` (L251). NOT statically assertable: that a given cut's concentration IS an artifact rather than a legitimate event-window cluster — that is the same read-and-judge terminal state as L165, which is why the flag is documented as blunt and the threshold is echoed in every result. NOT verifier-adjudicated: no independent `verifier` was dispatchable in this run's context, so this row is recorded as a re-runnable measurement, never as a verdict. |
| L258 | 2026-08-01 | **A verdict override that can only ever LOWER a cell is evidentially silent whenever an earlier branch already kills that cell — so the override's INPUT comparison must be published for every cell, not just where the override fires.** L255's mid-as-truth control was wired into `cell_verdict` after L256's worse-of-two exit treatment, and the flatten branch kills all ten of Q50's hold-ALIVE cells before the control is ever consulted: on today's tape the string `DEAD-by-zero-information-control` appears in ZERO cell verdicts even though the control reproduces the positive CI in 10 of 10 of those cells. A reader (or a future run) checking only the verdict column would conclude the control found nothing. The fix is not to reorder the kill ladder — the flatten kill is the honest one and ordering it first is correct — but to publish the control's own CI beside every cell's hold CI in the summary artifact and the printed report, so the evidence is visible independently of whether it was the proximate cause of death. Generalises beyond this probe: any last-resort gate (tick-magnitude, adverse-selection break-even, a placebo) is measured by what it SAYS, not by how often it is the executioner. | Q50/S68 L255 build, 2026-08-01 (research loop idle run, policy (a)) — `scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::cell_verdict` + `reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_summary.json` (`mid_as_truth` on all 35 cells; verdict set = {DEAD-by-CI, DEAD-by-magnitude, DEAD-by-adequacy}) | **test** — the publish-everywhere half is asserted, not just documented: `tests/test_q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::test_acceptance_l255_control_reproduces_every_hold_alive_cell_in_the_committed_ladder` opens with `assert all("mid_as_truth" in c for c in summary["cells"])` ("a control reported only where it is convenient is not a control") and `print_report` prints the hold-vs-control CI table for every cell.
